The B-path (`exec_and_collect`, a real box ran) keeps its legitimate `poll_meta → RunnerResultEnvelope` — untouched. **Verified LIVE end-to-end:** `dispatch_attest_offbox` completes and the finalized attested metrics round-trip the full submitted trajectory (tokens 100/50/10/5, 2 tool_calls grep×2, 2 model_turns, `cost_usd_micros` 0 from `None`). Adds two STANDING live-lane smokes (`crates/hugit-checks/tests/runner_apath_live_smoke.rs`, skip-in-CI / run when `HUGIT_RUNNER_HOST` set — the guard that was missing) and updates the A-path hermetic tests in `hugit-checks` + `hugit-cli` to the corrected acquire→submit→close flow. Follow-ups (noted): a FAILED off-box run needs an explicit exit input (v0 attests a completed run); the off-box close carries a live `result_binding_sig_v2` + `fabric_key_id:faa5b7726ccd2c52` — capturing it lights `✓ cas:` (owner-gated on cost-non-zero). Owns `crates/hugit-checks/src/runner/dispatch.rs` + `crates/hugit-checks/tests/runner_apath_live_smoke.rs` + `crates/hugit-cli/src/pr/dispatch.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test; both live smokes pass end-to-end against the CF fabricd).
- fix(serve): **the operator's `/v1/me/*` view no longer leaks a GDPR1-erased repo's slug (#91).** `AppState::me_repo_logs`'s operator branch returned EVERY loadable repo with no meta projection — so a repo with a terminal `repo.erased` tombstone (whose event log still loads; the tombstone is an append, not a rewrite) appeared in the operator's identity-scoped views while `authorize_read`/`authorize_write` 404 it on every OTHER path (read, git wire, tenant `/v1/me/*`). Erasure is terminal and must be gone from EVERY view, the operator's included. Now the operator branch consults the SAME cached meta the tenant branch uses (`repo_meta_cached`) and excludes ONLY `erased` — it still sees PRIVATE repos (the legitimate ops role; visibility is never filtered for an operator), just not erased ones. Found by the #74 fleet agent. 1 new test (operator sees a private repo but an erased repo is absent from `repos_for`) + the stale "operator branch is an unconditional bypass" note in the cache-invalidation test corrected. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/state.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test).
- feat(serve): **the engine token is now STATELESS + HMAC-signed — two engine instances are fungible on the auth plane (WP-B5, the `max_instances≥2` blocker #128).** The githugr TL's two-key `≥2` smoke caught a fungibility gap the refs fix (#272) did NOT close: `POST /v1/token` minted an opaque random token stored in an **in-process `Mutex<HashMap>` (`TokenStore`, single-host)**, and every authed call's `token_store.lookup` compared against THAT instance's map — so at `≥2` instances only the minting instance recognised a token → ~50% `401` on authed calls (`/v1/me/account` 11×200/9×401). Now the engine token is **self-describing + signed**: `mint` encodes the verified identity + expiry (`SignedClaims{user,org,fresh_auth,expires_at}`) as `hg1_<hex(payload)>.<hex(HMAC-SHA256)>`, and `lookup` **verifies the HMAC (constant-time `Mac::verify_slice`) + expiry STATELESSLY — no store read** — so ANY instance holding the shared `HUGIT_ENGINE_TOKEN_KEY` accepts ANY instance's token. **Chose stateless-signed over a D1-backed shared store deliberately:** a D1 (network) lookup per authed request would block the **single-threaded accept loop** on every call — the read-latency-DoS class this engine has been bitten by; the signed verify is a local HMAC (microseconds, no lock, no hop, no new infra). **Additive + zero-config for today's `max_instances=1`:** absent the env key, a **per-boot random key** is used → the exact pre-#128 single-host behaviour (a restart invalidates outstanding tokens, as the in-memory store already did); set the shared secret only at the `≥2` rollout and instances become fungible. The **revocation trade-off is already paid** — the engine token TTL is already `≤300s` and there is no individual engine-token revoke feature (only PATs), so the ≤5-minute TTL *is* the revocation window. The in-process store is retained ONLY for the admin session list (`list_for_org`, best-effort/single-host, unchanged); `lookup` no longer touches it. A legacy pre-#128 64-hex token (no `hg1_` prefix) → `Invalid` → the client re-mints (covers the rolling-deploy window). 9 new tests (cross-instance fungibility with a shared key; a different key rejects; tampered payload/signature rejected; legacy-hex + malformed-v1 shapes Invalid, never a panic; `from_env` shared-secret fungible vs distinct-secret rejected; absent-key single-host-but-functional; stateless expiry) + the existing mint/lookup/sweep/exchange tests updated for the signed shape. Cross-repo: the `engine_token` string is longer (still fully opaque `Bearer`) — flagged to the githugr TL to confirm no length assumption. **Deploy (not code):** set the SAME `HUGIT_ENGINE_TOKEN_KEY` on every instance before flipping `ENGINE_INSTANCE_COUNT=2`. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{token.rs,state.rs}` + this entry. Gate green.
- fix(ledger): **the free-text redaction engine no longer OVER-REDACTS long legitimate Org/branch/repo paths (#70b).** A long git ref like `refs/heads/some-very-long-descriptive-branch-name` or a deep repo path (`crates/hugit-ledger/src/secret_shape`) was replaced wholesale with `[REDACTED]` in the blob "Histórico" (and every `scrub`-ed view surface): `/` and `-` are token chars, so the WHOLE ref is one long run, and its Shannon entropy clears the free-text 4.0 floor — detector 5 (the high-entropy scan) fired on a human path. **The narrow, shape-based exemption** (`classify_path_token`, consulted in `high_entropy_token` AFTER the {40,64}-hex-digest + long-all-hex rules and the structural-secret guard): a token is spared ONLY when it is (1) path/slug charset `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` (base64's `+`/`=` excluded), (2) carries a `/`/`-` separator, (3) is word-dense NOT base64-dense — its class-transition density over the concatenated alphanumeric body (separator-placement invariant) is ≤ 0.5, AND (4) EVERY separator-delimited segment is a low-entropy word (not a structural secret, not a bare {40,64}-hex digest, not a ≥20-char all-hex run, and below the identifier-door's 4.5 per-segment entropy ceiling — reusing `IDENT_ENTROPY_THRESHOLD` so the two redaction layers stay consistent, not drift). **Detection is UNCHANGED for real secrets** — the classifier is fail-closed: a path-charset token that hides a hex digest / long-hex / high-entropy segment, or is base64-dense overall, is REDACTED, never spared. **Adversarial coverage:** the full secret-specimen set (`ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghs_`/`github_pat_`/`AKIA`/`xoxb-`/`glpat-`/`dop_v1_`/JWT `eyJ…`/`Bearer`/`sk-`/connection-string password/dense base64) still redacts BOTH standalone AND when disguised inside a path wrapper (`refs/heads/<secret>`, `src/vendor/<secret>/mod`); a dense base64 key split by `/`, hyphen-grouped, or sliced into 5-char `/` groups still redacts (density is measured over the concatenated body, so slicing cannot dilute it); the AWS example key with long same-class runs (low density) still redacts via the 4.5 per-segment entropy ceiling; a `+`/`=`-bearing base64 run never qualifies as a path. 15 new hermetic unit tests + 2 new proptest properties (long path/ref shapes survive; any credential disguised in a path context still redacts). Owns `crates/hugit-ledger/src/redact.rs` + `crates/hugit-ledger/tests/proptest_scrubber.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `-D warnings` + test, incl. proptests; `hugit-serve` `fmt` scrub consumer tests green).
- feat(serve): **Precomputed per-path blob-history index — a DEEP-history file's "Histórico" drawer now serves complete instead of empty (#70(a), the #69 follow-up).** The live "Histórico" walk (`hugit_proto::blob_history`) walks from HEAD *per path* under a 2 s wall-clock budget; for a file whose touches are DEEP in history, the many intervening non-touching commits exhaust that budget before a single touch is found → an EMPTY drawer (the known #69 gap). Now a precomputed **in-memory** index (`slug → (head, path → [BlobHistoryEntry])`, on `AppState.blob_history_index`) is built **OFF the single-threaded accept loop** — on a DETACHED thread at boot (`bootstrap_blob_history_indexes`, mirroring the cached clone-pack bootstrap) and after each push/delete (`spawn_blob_history_index_build`, so a moved HEAD gets a fresh index). The build (`hugit_proto::build_blob_history_index`) walks the history ONCE, diffing each commit against its first parent (`tree_diff_until`) to record every touched path's newest-first revisions in a single pass — so a deep file's touches are captured alongside the shallow ones. The blob read consults the index FIRST and **falls back to the live wall-clock-bounded walk on ANY miss** (no index built yet, a stale HEAD — a push moved the tip, refused via a HEAD-equality gate like clone-pack's `refset_sha`, or a path absent from the index) — a pure enhancement over the existing safety bound, never a replacement (behaviour on a miss is exactly today's). **Bounded build (never a boot-deadline or accept-loop DoS):** capped by `INDEX_BUILD_BUDGET` (120 s wall-clock) AND `MAX_INDEX_COMMITS` (20 000), each per-path list capped at `MAX_HISTORY_REVS`; the detached build never blocks boot (the ~18 s synchronous-CAS-probe crash-loop scar) and a build panic is `catch_unwind`-isolated. Redaction is unchanged — index entries are stored UNSCRUBBED and `scrub`bed at the read boundary exactly like the live walk (no bypass). 9 new tests: proto (deep-path recorded where a starved live walk is empty; per-path + max-commits + deadline bounds) + serve (deep-history-served-from-index vs an empty live walk; un-indexed-path + stale-HEAD fallbacks; index-served redaction; store HIT/MISS + single-flight build guard). Owns `crates/hugit-proto/src/read/history.rs` (+ `lib.rs` export) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/{blob_history_index.rs (new),state.rs,server.rs,git.rs,handlers/blob.rs,handlers/mod.rs,lib.rs}` + this entry. Gate green.
- perf(serve): **cache `repo.meta` per repo — closes the O(repos × log-size) per-request re-projection in `me_repo_logs`/`count_owned_repos` (task #74, W-METENANT scaling follow-up).** `AppState::me_repo_logs` (every `/v1/me/*` read) and `count_owned_repos` (the per-tenant provision-cap denominator) called `authz::project_repo_meta(&log)` — a full walk of the repo's ENTIRE event log to derive `{visibility, owner_tenant, erased}` — ONCE PER REPO PER REQUEST; on a per-tenant listing across many repos with growing histories this is a real scaling bottleneck on the single-threaded engine. New `AppState.repo_meta_cache` (`Arc<RwLock<HashMap<String, RepoMeta>>>`) makes the common case O(1): populated ONCE at boot for every loaded repo (`boot_populate_repo_meta_cache`, called from `from_env`), read via `repo_meta_cached(repo, log)` in `me_repo_logs`/`count_owned_repos`/`owned_repo_logs`. **The hard part — correctness on an authz-relevant projection, not just speed:** the cache is kept fresh by EVERY write path that can change a repo's projected meta — provisioning a new repo caches the freshly-persisted genesis meta directly (`write_provision::provision`, no redundant reload); a `repo.meta` visibility/owner-tenant update invalidates via `refresh_repo_meta_cache` right after `dispatch_repo_write`'s `with_write` succeeds; the GDPR1 `repo.erased` TOMBSTONE (`erasure::execute_account_erasure_inner`'s tombstone loop) refreshes the cache for that repo immediately — before the SAME cascade's TOCTOU re-assert or any concurrent request could observe a stale "not erased" answer (a stale cache here would leak private/erased content, not just slow-path a number). **Fail-safe by construction:** `repo_meta_cached` is the ONLY reader, and a cache MISS falls back to a LIVE `project_repo_meta` over the caller's already-loaded log — the exact pre-cache computation, so a coverage gap can only ever cost time, never a wrong answer; `refresh_repo_meta_cache` REMOVES (never leaves stale) an entry if its post-write reload somehow fails. **Deliberately NOT wired** into `authoritative_owned_repo_logs`/`erasure_repo_partition` — both are documented NOT-hot-read, rare-admin GDPR1 erasure-cascade paths; adding the cache there would buy no measurable perf and would add a second place to reason about freshness on the codebase's most security-sensitive path. 8 new hermetic tests: cache-hit matches live projection; cache-miss falls back to live (never a different answer); the security-critical case — a `repo.erased` tombstone written directly to the durable log is (as expected) still masked by a stale cache until `refresh_repo_meta_cache` runs, after which `me_repo_logs` stops serving the repo to its owner; the same invalidation proof for a visibility flip (private→public unlocks a foreign tenant's read); a failed reload removes rather than leaves a stale entry; boot population covers every loaded repo. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{state.rs,server.rs,writes/erasure.rs,writes/verbs/write_provision.rs}` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test).
- fix(serve): **GDPR1 erasure grace — a MIN-GRACE FLOOR so `grace=0` can never ship to prod (clw hardening, Track C.5).** The live-verify sets `HUGIT_ERASURE_GRACE_SECS=0` to execute an erasure immediately after staging it — but a prod deploy that forgot to restore the grace afterward would let the operator execute an IRREVERSIBLE erasure the instant it's requested, collapsing the two-authority cooling-off (a compromised session could stage AND have it executed with no dispute window). Now `erasure_grace_ms` clamps any sub-floor grace (incl. `0`) UP to `MIN_ERASURE_GRACE_SECS` (1h) — the smallest meaningful cooling-off — UNLESS the explicit, auditable escape hatch `HUGIT_ERASURE_ALLOW_BELOW_GRACE_FLOOR=1` is set (verify-only; a routine prod deploy never carries it). The clamp `eprintln!`s a warning so an operator sees a stray sub-floor grace was corrected. Pure `grace_secs_with_floor(configured, allow_below_floor)` (testable without env): `None`→7d default; sub-floor without opt-out→clamp to 1h; sub-floor WITH opt-out→raw (incl. 0); ≥floor→as-is. 1 hermetic test (all branches). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` + this entry. Gate green. (The remaining Track-C.5 item — restoring the prod grace after the live-verify — is a deploy-config action, not code; this floor makes forgetting it fail-safe.)

- feat(serve): **PAT `last_used_at` tracking — `GET /v1/me/account` now shows when each token was last used (finishes the user-managed PAT surface, #90).** The account read already listed the caller's OWN tokens + supported mint (`POST /v1/me/tokens`) + revoke (`DELETE /v1/me/tokens/{id}`), but `PatMetaVm.last_used_at` was hardcoded `0` ("slice 2b") — so a user couldn't spot an idle or leaked token, the whole point of user-managed PATs. Now `AppState.pat_last_used` (a `pat_id → Unix-ms` map) is updated on every successful `resolve_pat_from_auth` (keyed by the non-secret `pat_id` derived from the matched candidate via `pat_id_of_secret`; monotonic `max` so an out-of-order call never rewinds), and `build_me_account` merges it onto each projected PAT. **Cheap on the accept loop** — an in-memory map write, NO durable I/O (a `pat.used` append per auth would be a log-bloat + accept-loop-latency DoS). **Honest limitation (documented on the contract field):** in-memory only → resets on engine restart (`0` = "not seen used since boot", NOT necessarily "never used") + per-instance under `max_instances>1`; a durable off-loop flush (like the B5 ref refresher) is the tracked follow-up. Secret/hash never leave the engine (keyed + surfaced by the opaque `pat_id` only). 2 new tests (the merge onto the projected PAT with an idle-token-stays-0 case; the monotonic record + snapshot) + the existing account tests updated. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{state.rs,server.rs,handlers/me_account.rs,writes/verbs/write_token.rs}` + `crates/hugit-http-contracts/src/account.rs` (doc-only) + this entry. Gate green.

- feat(serve): **B5 read-after-write ref refresh — two engine instances are now FUNGIBLE (the `max_instances>1` fix; clw-designed 2026-07-06).** Ref reads were per-instance in-memory (`LiveRefs`), so a push to instance A left instance B advertising a STALE tip until B rebooted — the HA blocker. Now a dedicated background thread (`spawn_refs_refresh_loop`, started at boot in CAS mode) reloads every loaded repo's durable `<tenant>/<repo>/refs.json` every `REFS_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS` (**2s**, clw-signed-off for 2 instances) and installs it via the new `LiveRefs::replace` — so B picks up A's push/delete within ~2s (a full replace, so adds AND cross-instance deletes propagate). **Strictly OFF the single-threaded accept loop:** the thread clones the per-repo `LiveRefs` Arc handles BEFORE any R2 I/O (never holds a lock across a network read) and only writes the shared map (exactly what the push hot-swap does) — the accept loop NEVER blocks on an R2 read (the read-latency-DoS class we've been bitten by). **Design choice:** a periodic background refresher (Option-2 short-TTL) over a per-request-triggered one — same bounded-staleness outcome, simpler, and ZERO accept-loop interaction (stricter on clw's "never block the loop" axis). **Safe, not just fast (the load-bearing invariant):** a stale advertise is UX-only, never a lost update — every durable ref mutation rides the receive-pack log compare-and-swap + the conditional If-Match `refs.json` PUT, so a push on a stale base is rejected non-fast-forward and the client retries (locked by a new test: after the refresh installs another instance's advance, a stale-base push is `non-fast-forward`). `refresh_repo_refs_once` is FAIL-SAFE — an absent manifest / read fault / malformed JSON keeps the current cache (never installs an empty/corrupt map). 5 hermetic tests (install adds+updates+drops; absent/fault/malformed keep the cache; the refresh→stale-base-rejected invariant). NO-OP in Local/git-dir mode. **Upgrade path:** a zero-staleness conditional `GET If-None-Match` (Option 1, we already capture the ETag) before widening past `max_instances=2`. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{state.rs,git.rs}` + this entry. Gate green. **Activation (with the `/readyz` fail-closed below) is clw+githugr-coordinated** — `max_instances≥2` + the dormant health-router (#120) + the probe-grace runbook, flipped in lockstep.

- fix(serve): **`/readyz` is now FAIL-CLOSED (WP-B5 fungibility pre-condition).** `GET /readyz` returned `200 {"ready":true}` UNCONDITIONALLY — a cold/booting instance advertised itself healthy, so a health router would send it traffic and it would serve stale/empty while the lazy-CAS boot deps warm. Now readiness is gated on the boot `cas_batch_read` self-probe (`engine_ready`): `ok …`/`empty-index` (the CAS batch plane is serviceable) OR `unprobed` (no lazy-CAS dep — Local/git-dir) → **200 `ready:true`**; `probing` (the detached self-probe has not landed — still booting) OR `err:…` (the CAS plane failed) → **503 `ready:false`**. The full field set (`git_serving`/`git_repos`/`version`/`cas_batch_read`/`clonepack`) is returned in BOTH states so a consumer sees WHY (e.g. `cas_batch_read:"probing"`). Fast + deterministic — reads the in-memory probe cell only (no I/O), so `/readyz` never hangs. This lets a health router route AROUND a booting/broken instance — the hard pre-condition for `max_instances>1` (paired with the refs.json read-after-write fungibility fix, the next B5 slice). 4 hermetic tests (the predicate's 5 cases; `/readyz` 503-while-probing→200-when-ok; 503-on-err; 200-for-a-non-CAS engine). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` + this entry. Gate green. **⚠️ DEPLOY-COORDINATION (not code):** the platform/engine-worker must NOT use `/readyz` as a RESTART-triggering liveness probe (a boot-window 503 would crash-loop) — verify the health-check grace ≥ the ~15-20s probe window, or deploy this WITH the `max_instances≥2` + health-router rollout. Do NOT deploy blind to the single pinned prod engine.

- feat(contracts+serve): **GDPR1 DSR legitimacy id — `AccountEraseReq.dsr_id` (additive, optional) captured on the `erasure.requested` record.** githugr holds the `/_internal/dsr/anchor` duty (it threads a `dsr_id` from CoreLink); the executor reads that id back off the request record to authorize the physical CAS-erase seam (`{dsr_id, tenant}` — a leaked erase key alone cannot delete). New optional field `dsr_id: Option<String>` on `AccountEraseReq` (`#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if)]` → a legacy `{"confirm":…}` body still deserializes; back-compat proven). **Optional until the executor is live:** today's honest "solicitado" staging does not require it (githugr flag-gates the anchor call), so a request may omit it; the executor will require a valid `dsr_id` before any physical deletion. `write_account_erase` captures it verbatim onto the `erasure.requested` payload WHEN present + non-empty (an empty id is treated as absent — no bogus legitimacy token). Tests: round-trip + legacy-body compat, present-id captured, empty/absent-id omitted. Owns `crates/hugit-http-contracts/src/write_requests.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/verbs/write_account_erase.rs` + this entry. Gate green. (Pins clw's DSR wiring: field = `dsr_id`, body-level, optional-until-executor-live.)
- feat(serve): **GDPR1 slice-2 — the operator-execute ROUTE (bar #4 complete; the executor is now callable, NOT yet live-enabled).** `POST /v1/account/erase/execute` drives the irreversible erasure cascade over a subject-staged standing request (design **D1** operator-execute, clw-audited 2026-07-06). Fail-closed at every gate: **operator-only** (`is_operator` → a tenant/anon gets the uniform no-oracle 404; the operator executes a standing lawful request, NEVER originates one — the two-authority protection the grace window exists for); **erase seam configured** (`state.erase_config` absent → 404, route disabled + presence not disclosed); **step-up** (fresh session OR the operator's `X-Step-Up` → else 403); **subject from the STANDING record** (`erasure::read_standing_erasure_request` reads the latest governing `erasure.requested` — the SUBJECT + `dsr_id` come from THAT record, Part-1 subject-authenticated, NEVER a caller arg; superseded by a later `erasure.cancelled`/`erasure.executed` → dropped; absent → 404, no god-erase); **grace elapsed** (`HUGIT_ERASURE_GRACE_SECS`, owner knob, default 7d → refuse before → 403); **DSR legitimacy** (no `dsr_id` → 403, never a physical delete without the githugr-anchored id); **CAS mode** (not CAS → 503). Then drives `execute_account_erasure_composed` over the subject. Threads the three clw must-fixes: (a) subject-from-record (the #256 free-string god-erase is closed); (b) grace/cancelled gate (v0 grace-only — no cancel verb; the `ERASURE_CANCELLED_KIND` scan is future-proofing, the operator is the cancel authority); (c) the **enumerate-claim TOCTOU** re-assert now lives in the executor — immediately before the `erasure.executed` claim it re-plans the durable owned set and downgrades to `erasure.partial` (re-runnable, `outstanding: owned-repo-appeared-mid-cascade`) if a repo appeared since the drive, never over-claiming `executed`. AppState boot-wiring: `erase_config: Option<EraseConfig>` (from `EraseConfig::from_env`, fail-closed; `EraseConfig` is `Clone` + built per-call since `HttpCasErase` is `!Sync`) + `cas_r2_read()`/`cas_tenant()` accessors (one R2 bucket, `HUGIT_SERVE_CAS_TENANT_ID`). **NOT yet live-enabled:** the route is disabled unless the erase seam is configured, and the whole cascade rides clw's INDEPENDENT COLD ADVERSARIAL AUDIT before any enablement + the erase-key issuance. 14 new hermetic tests (7 route gates: non-operator→404, seam-absent→404, step-up→403, no-standing→404, grace→403, no-dsr→403, not-CAS→503; 6 standing-reader: subject/dsr/time, dsr-absent, superseded-by-executed/cancelled, never-requested, re-request-after-cancel; 1 TOCTOU re-assert predicate). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{server.rs,state.rs,writes/erasure.rs}` + this entry. Gate green. **Deploy follow-up (not code):** add `/v1/account/erase/execute` to the engine-worker forward-list before live-verify.

- feat(serve): **GDPR1 slice-2 — the REAL HTTP `CasEraseTransport` (bar #4, network seam; NOT route-wired).** `erasure::HttpCasErase` is the only code that opens a socket to CoreLink's live internal erase seam, mirroring the ureq + SSRF-allowlist discipline of the session-exchange client. `erase` POSTs `{base}/_internal/cas/<tenant>/<digest>/erase` with the internal-auth key sent **RAW** in `x-corelink-internal-auth` (NEVER a Bearer — a Bearer is 401 on this seam) and body `{tenant, dsr_id, reason}`; the confirmed contract (corelink-server #634) answers **410 Gone** (fresh delete) or **200 AlreadyErased** (idempotent replay) — BOTH record the digest as CONFIRMED-gone; any other outcome (401/403/404/5xx/transport) is FAIL-CLOSED `Err`, never a silent success/recorded-gone. `is_gone` reflects that recorded gone-truth directly with **NO independent network read**: the corelink-server TL confirmed (2026-07-05) the erase's **410 is durably read-consistent** (the handler deletes the R2 bytes + upserts the `cas_tombstone` D1 row before returning, and the CAS read consults the same primary D1 → read-your-write), so the erase response IS the durable physical-GC proof and a separate fetch-by-digest verify would be redundant (and would need a `cas:r` PAT on a different data-plane route, not the internal-auth key) — the guessed `GET /_internal/cas/...` was wrong on both path and auth, and is dropped. `EraseConfig::from_env` reads `CORELINK_ERASE_URL` + `CORELINK_ERASE_AUTH_KEY` FAIL-CLOSED via a pure `validate` (absent URL → no transport → executor stays `partial`; empty/scheme-less/untrusted-host → `Err`; a URL WITHOUT a key → `Err`, a half-configured seam never boots). `EraseConfig` carries a REDACTING `Debug` (the key never appears in a log/panic). 10 hermetic tests (4 config: absent→none, url-without-key→fail-closed, untrusted-host→SSRF-reject, trusted→ok+slash-trim; 6 wire via a `tiny_http` mock seam: 410→gone+RAW-key-verified, 200-AlreadyErased→gone, 401→503-not-recorded, 5xx→503, and `is_gone` reflects only the erase with no socket — un-erased→not-gone, recorded→gone, one-recorded-does-not-mark-others). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs` (+ `token::extract_host` made `pub(crate)`) + this entry. Gate green.

- feat(serve): **GDPR1 slice-2 — the executor COMPOSITION (clw's bar #3).** `erasure::execute_account_erasure_composed(state, digests, account, chain, at, erase, cas_tenant, dsr_id)` ties the three read-side bars into the single call the route drives: derive the durable subject/surviving repo partition (bar #1, `AppState::erasure_repo_partition`), read each repo's referenced digests (`digests: &dyn RepoDigestSource` — the real `R2OidIndexDigests` in prod, a double in tests), compute `subject − surviving`, and drive `execute_account_erasure_with_erase` over EXACTLY that set. **The exact-superset guarantee bar #3 requires:** the digests physically erased are PRECISELY the computed exclusive set — a surviving user's shared object is structurally impossible to erase (subtracted before the drive ever sees it), and an EMPTY exclusive set is a LEGITIMATE `executed` erasure (tombstone the repos, delete nothing physical, claim executed honestly). FAIL-CLOSED end to end: the partition aborts on an indeterminate durable listing (bar #1, 503) and the digest read aborts on any R2 fault/malformed index (bar #2, 503) — nothing is erased under an incomplete surviving set (that is why `digests` is a trait object, not constructed inline). 3 hermetic tests (exact-superset: erases exactly `subject − surviving`, never a shared/foreign digest; empty-exclusive → executed=legitimate-retention, nothing erased; surviving-repo read-fault → 503 with not a single digest erased). NOT route-wired + NOT live: the operator-execute route + the 3 must-fixes (principal-derive, requested/grace/cancelled gate, enumerate-claim TOCTOU), the real HTTP `CasEraseTransport`, and the DSR-consume are the remaining slice-2 pieces — all behind clw's final re-audit; nothing deletes until then. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs` + this entry. Gate green.

- feat(serve): **GDPR1 slice-2 — the real R2 oid-index `RepoDigestSource` reader (clw's bar #2).** `erasure::R2OidIndexDigests` (holds a `&dyn R2Get`) reads a repo's `<tenant>/<repo>/oid-index.json` and collects its blake3 VALUES (the CAS digests the repo references). FAIL-CLOSED — the partition's safety rests on it: an R2 read fault OR a malformed index → `Err` (503, NEVER a partial digest set — a shrunk set on a surviving repo would delete a retained object); an ABSENT index (`get_object → None`, a genuine 404 — a repo with no pushed objects) → the EMPTY set (references nothing, safe on both sides). The absent-vs-fault distinction is load-bearing and is exactly what `R2Get::get_object` provides (404→None, fault→Err). 4 hermetic tests (values collected; absent→empty-not-fault; malformed→503; read-fault→503). Still not wired to the executor (the composition + route are the remaining slice-2 pieces). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs` + this entry. Gate green.

- feat(serve): **GDPR1 slice-2 — the `surviving_repos` durable derivation (clw's #1 blast-radius bar).** The exclusive-digest partition (#268) is only safe if the SURVIVING set is COMPLETE — an omitted surviving repo → a shared digest mis-classified exclusive → a retained user's data erased. New `AppState::erasure_repo_partition(subject) → (subject_repos, surviving_repos)` derives BOTH from the SAME durable authoritative candidate set as `authoritative_owned_repo_logs` (`list_repo_slugs()` — the durable R2 listing — ∪ the loaded overlay) with the SAME fail-closed discipline: an unloadable candidate (5xx) propagates `Err` (503, never a partial surviving set); a 404 (vanished between list+load) is skipped from both sides (a gone repo references nothing). Two fail-safe choices: an UNOWNED repo (no `owner_tenant`) is classified SURVIVING (retain on ambiguity — never delete), and the subject side is asserted equal to `authoritative_owned_repo_logs` (no drift between the two derivations). Test seeds subject-owned + other-owned repos → subject/surviving split + the authoritative-parity assertion. NOT yet a caller of the partition (the real oid-index `RepoDigestSource` reader + the composition + the operator-execute route + the 3 must-fixes are the remaining slice-2 pieces, all behind clw's final re-audit — nothing deletes until then). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/state.rs` + this entry. Gate green.

- feat(serve): **GDPR1 executor — the CAS-erase transport seam + the physically-GC'd `executed` path (premise-corrected; hermetic, still behind clw's re-audit).** The corelink-server TL corrected a load-bearing wrong premise: CoreLink CAS is keyed PER-TENANT (not cross-tenant deduped), so cross-CoreLink-tenant physical sharing is impossible; exclusivity is intra-hugit (a surviving user = a **legitimate retention**, not a residual-risk), and account-EXCLUSIVE digests ARE physically deletable via the LIVE seam `POST /_internal/cas/<tenant>/<hash>/erase` → 410 Gone. New `erasure::CasEraseTransport` trait (`erase` + `is_gone`/410-verify, both fail-closed — an uncertain outcome is never a false "gone") abstracts that seam so the executor is HERMETICALLY testable. `execute_account_erasure_with_erase(...)` drives it: after tombstoning the owned repos, it erases EACH exclusive digest + asserts each is 410-gone, and claims `erasure.executed` ONLY when every one is erased+verified — else `erasure.partial` (records `digests_erased`, NEVER over-claims); a hard erase fault → 503 before any claim (idempotent retry converges). The classic `execute_account_erasure` (no transport) stays `partial` (obligation unmet). Corrected the now-false module doc + the cross-tenant-dedup premise. 4 hermetic tests (mock transport: exclusive-digests→executed; empty-exclusive→executed=legitimate-retention; not-410-verified→partial; erase-fault→503-no-claim). NOT route-wired + NOT live: the REAL HTTP transport, the exclusive-digest partition from the manifest graph, the `CORELINK_ERASE_AUTH_KEY`, the DSR `dsr_id` origin, and the requested/grace/cancelled + enumerate-claim-TOCTOU route guards are slice-2 (clw re-audits the reworked irreversible-delete path first). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs` + this entry. Gate green.
- feat(serve): **GDPR1 executor — the exclusive-digest PARTITION (slice-2's #1 blast-radius; pure + fail-closed + hermetic).** With the CAS tenant pinned to the SHARED `d863fafb` (verified from the write path — no per-user keying), erasing an account is NOT a whole-tenant wipe: the executor may physically erase ONLY the account-EXCLUSIVE digests (referenced by the subject but by NO surviving user); a shared digest is a legitimate retention. New `erasure::RepoDigestSource` trait (a repo → its referenced blake3 digests = the VALUES of `<tenant>/<repo>/oid-index.json`; abstracted so the partition is hermetic, real R2-read is slice-2 wiring) + `partition_exclusive_digests(src, tenant, subject_repos, surviving_repos)` = `subject − surviving`. **FAIL-CLOSED with the correct direction:** an enumeration fault on ANY repo → `Err` (503, erase nothing) — critically, a fault reading a SURVIVING repo aborts, because a shrunk surviving set would mis-classify a shared digest as exclusive → delete a retained object (the worst outcome). The caller owns passing a COMPLETE authoritative `surviving_repos` (every non-subject repo, the planner's B1 discipline). 4 hermetic tests: exclusive-only (shared retained), no-surviving→all-exclusive, fault-on-surviving-aborts (the critical case), fault-on-subject-aborts. NOT wired to the executor/route yet (that + the real oid-index read are the remaining slice-2 pieces, behind clw's combined re-audit). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs` + this entry. Gate green.
- fix(contracts): **PAT timestamps are Unix MILLISECONDS, not seconds — corrected the `PatMetaVm` doc/example (silent wire-unit drift).** The engine is ms-native (`created_at: now_ms()`, `expires_at = at + ttl_secs*1000`, `is_expired` compares `now_ms >= expires_at`), and `CreatedTokenVm.expires_at` was already doc'd "Unix ms" — but `PatMetaVm.created_at`/`last_used_at` were doc'd "Unix **seconds**" with a seconds example (`1_720_000_000`). A consumer (githugr) reconciling to seconds renders every PAT timestamp ~1000× off (far-future dates). Corrected the docs to "Unix ms", the example to a 13-digit ms value, and added a contract-pin assertion (`created_at >= 1e12`) so the unit can't silently drift back. Serde never caught it (both are `u64`) — the classic hugit-http-contracts ⇄ consumer silent-drift class. Owns `crates/hugit-http-contracts/src/account.rs` + this entry.
- feat(checks): **transcribe the FROZEN agent-exec seam DTOs (cost-killer path B) byte-identical from corelink-runners #290 + the drift tripwire.** The Runners TL ratified (B) exec-server drive and froze the 3 agent-exec DTOs; hugit mirrors them so the wire can't silently drift (the class that hit the 4 lease DTOs). New `runner::{AgentExecRequest (argv/ordered-env/workdir/timeout_ms), AgentExecAck (lease_id/step_id/accepted), AgentExecResult (step_id/exit_code/stdout/stderr/duration_ms/truncated), AgentSpec}` — for hugit's OFF-BOX §13 agent loop to drive an egress-enabled, NON-memoized agent box (`POST /v1/leases/{id}/agent-exec` → ack; `GET …/{step_id}` → result), then close with the provider-`/usage` `cost_usd_micros` + §13 envelope (both UNCHANGED, already frozen). `conformance/{AgentExecRequest,AgentExecAck,AgentExecResult}.json` committed byte-identical (sha256 in `conformance/manifest.sha256`, matched to the Runners TL's published hashes b16a5bd/4e7522e/759471d); the x4 `VECTORS` set + `shasum -c` pin them, and `wire_conformance.rs` asserts the REQUEST hugit sends round-trips byte-exact + the ACK/RESULT parse (liberal-in). The `agent`-on-acquire field + the transport methods ride the next slice (they follow the fabric's live routes); the §13 agent LOOP itself remains the P2 deferral. Owns `crates/hugit-checks/src/runner/{lease_client.rs,mod.rs}` + `crates/hugit-checks/tests/wire_conformance.rs` + `crates/hugit-invariants/x4/tests/acceptance_x4_wire.rs` + `conformance/*` + this entry.
- feat(serve): **clone-pack build-window legibility — a full-clone cache MISS answers a retryable 503, never a silent slow-walk; `/readyz` surfaces `clonepack`.** During the ~minutes background clone-pack assembly at boot, a full `git clone` of a cache-backed (CAS) repo used to fall open to the whole-closure slow walk — a single-thread latency DoS that the io-deadline abandoned as a silent empty/hung clone. Now the cache lookup is classified (`clone_pack::CloneCacheLookup::{Hit,Absent,Stale}`): **Hit** streams the cached pack; **Absent** (no pointer yet — building, or a transient read) returns a **retryable 503 + `Retry-After`** (`respond_clone_pack_building`) so `git`/the client backs off + retries with a clear message instead of a broken clone; **Stale** (a moved ref) still FALLS OPEN to the walk — byte-correct and avoids wedging a repo unclonable until the next push rebuilds the pack (e.g. after `push --delete`). Only a cache-backed repo's full clone takes this path (`takes_clone_cache_path`); a seam-less repo (GIT_DIR/small) and any bounded fetch walk as before. `/readyz` gains a cheap `clonepack` field (`"idle"` | `"building:<slug>,…"`) from the in-memory build-guard set — NO R2 read, so the liveness probe stays fast — so a consumer sees WHY a clone is 503ing. Tests: the `Hit`/`Absent`/`Stale` classification, `takes_clone_cache_path` routing, the `clonepack` snapshot, and the integration `full_clone_absent_cache_is_503_building_not_a_dos_walk` (+ the existing stale-cache-falls-open test stays green). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{clone_pack.rs, git.rs, state.rs, server.rs}` + this entry. Gate green.
- feat(serve): **PAT git-auth WIRING (WP-#90 slice 2b) — the resolver wired into the live auth hot path, behind `HUGIT_SERVE_PAT_AUTH` (default OFF).** The last coded step of user-managed PATs: a PAT now authenticates git/API when the flag is on. A NEW auth surface, so it ships DISABLED behind an adversarial review (build → review → enable, like the GDPR executor); NOT deployed. `AppState.pat_index` (`sha256(secret) → PatAuth`, `Arc<RwLock>`) built by a **detached** boot-scan of `_accounts/*` (off the boot path — a synchronous per-account verified R2 fetch would blow the container startup deadline as accounts grow, the chunk-256 class; mirrors the CAS self-probe), merge-in so a create during warm-up survives; create/revoke refresh the index inline (immediate mint/revoke, no reboot). Credential extraction accepts `Bearer <secret>` AND the git-CLI `Basic base64(user:secret)` password (`candidate_pat_secrets` + a hand-rolled, panic-safe standard base64 decoder — no dep churn in the deny-multiple-versions workspace). Tier-1.5 in `two_tier_auth_ctx`/`clone_principal`/receive-pack (before the dev-token → a PAT is NEVER the operator, resolves only to its `clerk:{org}:{user}` owner). The write-scope gate: `write_auth` → 403 `SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT` for a `repo:read`-only PAT on every `/v1` mutation + the receive-pack push (revoke deliberately NOT gated — it only reduces access); a `repo:write` PAT / session / dev credential passes. Fail-closed multi-instance boot guard (the index is single-instance-authoritative — gated on B5 for `max_instances>1`). **A PAT cannot mint tokens** (`AuthCtx.is_pat` → `POST /v1/me/tokens` refuses a PAT with 403 `PAT_CANNOT_MINT`). **clw's independent-review must-fix (the 1 gate on APPROVE):** a server-side max-TTL cap in `token_create` (`MAX_TTL_MS` = 90 days) — `ttl_secs == 0` ("never") is CLAMPED to the ceiling + any larger request capped, so `expires_at` is ALWAYS non-zero → **no PAT is ever never-expiring** → the boot-warm-up/crash eviction-skip window is time-bounded, closing the otherwise unbounded revocation-evasion. A self-run 3-auditor adversarial sweep (auth-bypass, index-lifecycle, panic/DoS) returned SOUND; the two findings (sync boot build + write-PAT self-proliferation) fixed at root. New `error::EngineErr::{scope_insufficient, pat_cannot_mint}` (403). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{server.rs, git.rs, state.rs, error.rs, writes/verbs/write_token.rs}` + `docs/design/2026-07-05-pat-git-auth-wire.md` + this entry. Gate green. Until the flag is enabled (post-review + redeploy), a PAT lists/revokes but does not yet authenticate git.
- feat(serve)+docs: **PAT git-auth wire (WP-#90 slice 2b) — the PURE auth-resolution foundation + the decided wiring design (a new auth surface, so the hot-path wiring ships behind adversarial review).** The resolver that turns a presented PAT secret into its owning principal + scopes, built + adversarially unit-tested, WITHOUT yet touching the live auth hot path (the wiring is the reviewed follow-on, treated like the GDPR execution cascade). `writes::verbs::write_token`: `PatAuth {principal, scopes, expires_at}` (the token owner's FULL `clerk:{org}:{user}` — structurally NEVER the operator), `index_account_log` (build the `secret_hash → PatAuth` index for an account log, skipping revoked), `resolve_pat(index, raw_secret, now_ms)` (fast-reject non-`ghgr_pat_` bearers without hashing; hash → lookup → expiry-check → `Some(owner+scopes)`, else `None`) + `PatAuth::{principal_chain, is_expired, can_write}` (the write gate). **A double-wrap bug the adversarial test caught:** `pat.created.user` stores the FULL principal, so `PatAuth` holds it verbatim, NOT re-wrapped as `clerk:{org}:{user}`. 5 new adversarial tests (live PAT → its clerk owner, never operator; wrong-prefix/unknown/revoked → None; expired → None at the boundary; read-only can't write; per-user isolation + the index stores hashes-not-secrets). The **decided wiring design** `docs/design/2026-07-05-pat-git-auth-wire.md`: the in-memory index on `AppState` (boot-scan `_accounts/` → O(1) hot-path lookup, no per-request R2; refresh on create/revoke; single-instance-authoritative, rides B5 for multi-instance), Bearer + **HTTP Basic** extraction (the git-CLI reality — the PAT is the Basic password), Tier-1.5 insertion in `two_tier_auth`/`clone_principal`/receive-pack (before the dev-token → a PAT is never operator), the `can_write` scope gate, + a 9-item adversarial checklist for the pre-live review. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/verbs/write_token.rs` + `docs/design/…` + this entry. Gate green. NOT wired to the live auth path; a PAT still does not authenticate git until the reviewed wiring lands.
- test(cli): **harden the N-2 reconcile perf test against runner contention — MIN-of-N per size (closes the recurring #88 flake).** `reconcile_in_sync_scales_linearly_not_quadratically` took a SINGLE timed probe per size + asserted the ratio, on the false theory that contention "slows both proportionally, so the ratio is invariant to load". A probe is slowed INDEPENDENTLY, so a fast baseline + a spike-hit larger run inflated the ratio past the 5× band (flaked at 4.19×/5.79×/7.87× on the shared self-hosted runner — pure machine noise, never an algorithmic regression; it blocked 3 unrelated PRs). Fix: measure each size as the MIN of 3 runs — the fastest run is the least-contended, closest-to-true-cost estimate, so the ratio-of-mins reflects the algorithm not the scheduler. Owns `crates/hugit-cli/tests/acceptance_n2_reconcile_perf.rs` + this entry.
- feat(serve): **user-managed PATs — the durable per-account token store + `POST /v1/me/tokens` (create) + `DELETE /v1/me/tokens/{id}` (revoke) + real `me/account.pats` projection (WP-#90 slice 2a).** The create/revoke/surface half of user-managed personal access tokens (owner-decided 2026-07-05: PATs are a feature — needed for real git CLI). **Storage reuses the GDPR account seam:** PATs live on the per-account event log (`_accounts/{org}`) as append-only `pat.created`/`pat.revoked` records; the token **SECRET NEVER touches the log** — only its SHA-256 hash (ADR-0002), and the raw secret (`ghgr_pat_<64 hex>`, OS-CSPRNG) is returned **exactly once** on create. **Identity + isolation:** a PAT belongs to a specific USER within an org (`clerk:{org}:{user}`); `project_pats` returns only the caller's OWN live (non-revoked) tokens' metadata — never the hash, never another user's tokens (cross-user isolation within an org). **`POST /v1/me/tokens`** (`write_token::token_create`): mints → hashes → appends `pat.created` via a bounded account-log CAS, returns `201 {id,name,secret,scopes,created_at,expires_at}` (secret once); NOT idempotent (each call a distinct token, mirrors GitHub); operator/anon refused 401 (no god/anon token); invalid scope / empty name → 400; per-account cap (`MAX_PATS_PER_ACCOUNT=50`) → 429. **`DELETE /v1/me/tokens/{id}`** (`token_revoke`): appends `pat.revoked`; **idempotent** (re-revoke = no-op); a foreign/unknown id → 404 (no cross-user oracle). **`GET /v1/me/account`** now projects the caller's real `pats` from their account log (was `[]`). Scopes v0: `repo:read`/`repo:write`; secret prefix `ghgr_pat_`; no step-up, optional expiry (`ttl_secs`, 0=never) — flagged to githugr for confirmation. 10 new hermetic tests (secret-once + only-the-hash-stored; per-user isolation; revoke removes + idempotent; foreign/unknown revoke 404; operator/anon 401; invalid scope/name 400; me/account projection + cross-user; the create/revoke route wiring). Owns `crates/hugit-http-contracts/src/account.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/verbs/{mod.rs,write_token.rs}` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/handlers/me_account.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/tests/write_integration.rs` + this entry. Fixed (planner model): the CAS leg is SPLIT — `DisclosureLeg::CasShared` (shared objects, defensible disclose — kept) vs `CasGcObligation` (account-exclusive, physical GC REQUIRED); with the CAS-GC seam not wired in v0 `ErasurePlan::is_launch_blocked()` fires LOUDLY as a separately-tracked go-live blocker (the physical GC is a CoreLink server/CAS-TL cross-repo seam, relayed by clw — a HARD pre-launch blocker under the no-waiver bar), never a soft disclosure. **Nits:** the planner self-refuses a `!is_honest()` plan; `ResidualDisclosure.leg` is a closed `DisclosureLeg` enum (no planner/executor drift). 10 new hermetic tests (durable-but-unloaded enumeration; ListObjectsV2 XML key/token parsing; Local slug listing excludes account logs; the identity-divergence 400; the CAS-leg split + launch-blocked). the operator may only EXECUTE a request the SUBJECT itself staged — Part-1's `derive_owner_tenant` guarantees `erasure.requested` subject == the requester — never mint one, no god-erase); (D2) the five real legs — the account's repos tombstoned by a TERMINAL `repo.erased` record on each append-only log (NEVER a rewrite; physical GC is a CoreLink-owned seam), plus the github-mirror + context-store P2 seams; (D3) fail-closed durability ordering (`erasure.executed` appended LAST, only after every durable tombstone — the receive-pack `ok ⇒ durable` law, no `executed`-without-deletion); (D4) idempotent replay = no-op + irreversibility guard (no un-tombstone) + partial-progress converges; plus a 10-item adversarial-audit checklist **clw runs INDEPENDENTLY before any live enablement**. **Slice 1 — the read-only planner** (`crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/erasure.rs`): `plan_account_erasure(state, account) -> ErasurePlan` enumerates every repo owned by the subject (the authoritative in-memory `owned_repo_logs` projection — the `(slug,log)` twin of `count_owned_repos`, fail-closed `None` → 503 on an indeterminate enumeration so a subject's repo is never missed), marks each `already_erased` (idempotency-aware via a terminal `repo.erased` scan) or pending, and attaches the v0 honest NON-EMPTY residual-risk disclosures (cas-dedup / github-mirror / context-store; `is_honest()` fails closed on an empty disclosure, mirroring X7/X12's `is_honestly_resolved`). - feat(serve): **GDPR1 account-erase — the account-scoped persistence seam + the top-level `POST /v1/account/erase` route (Part 1 of the WP is now WIRED, staging-only; no deletion code).** Completes the request-staging half of the GDPR1 account-erase WP against the decided+corrected design: a user can now request erasure of THEIR OWN account over the wire, durably recorded to a dedicated per-account log. **The design CORRECTION this ships (caught during the handler build, before it became a bug):** the first cut keyed the account log at the repo WriteStore `_erasure/{slug}` relying on the `/` to make `is_safe_repo_slug` reject it (non-servable) — but `AppState::persist` ALSO gates on `is_safe_repo_slug`, so the very property that made the key non-servable made it non-**persistable**. Fixed at root with a **dedicated account store seam** (NOT the repo LogSink): `state.rs` gains `is_safe_account_slug` (the traversal-safe Clerk-org shape `[a-z0-9-]`, ≤64 — a malformed org can never splice an `_accounts/../evil.json` key), `LogSource::{fetch_account,persist_account}` keyed under the reserved `<tenant>/_accounts/{slug}.json` sub-prefix (Local + R2, structurally OUTSIDE the repo namespace → never served/clone-able as a repo), and `AppState::{load_account_log,persist_account_log}` — **load-or-create** (an absent account log is the empty world with a create-only `CasToken::Absent`, so the first request seeds the genesis), chain-verified on read, fail-closed (404) on an unsafe slug. **The account write-door** `writes::with_account_write` (over the new `AccountLogSink` trait) is the account-scoped twin of `with_write`: it SHARES the same idempotency ledger + body-hash replay guard + bounded CAS retry loop, with two deliberate differences — NO repo-ownership gate (the subject is the caller's OWN account, derived by the verb; a caller can only erase itself) and load-or-create (never a repo-404). STEP-UP is still enforced here (`"erasure"` ∈ `STEP_UP_VERBS`) — the top-level route skips the repo door, so this is the ONLY step-up chokepoint. **The route** `["v1","account","erase"]` (`server.rs::dispatch_account_erase`): two-tier auth → derive the subject account from the principal (`derive_owner_tenant` refuses operator/anon → 401, no god-erase / anon-erase, BEFORE any store touch) → step-up (fresh Clerk session or the dev-only `X-Step-Up`) → Idempotency-Key (≤256B) → `with_account_write` running the pure `write_account_erase` verb. 13 new hermetic tests (seam load-or-create + CAS round-trip + traversal-refusal + `is_safe_account_slug` matrix; the account door's genesis/step-up-403/idempotent-replay/wrong-confirm-400/same-key-race; the route's operator-401 + anon-401). NEVER deletes — the irreversible X7/X12 EXECUTION cascade (`erasure.requested → erasure.executed`) is the REMAINING Part 2, gated behind the clw independent adversarial audit before it can tombstone real data; live-verify needs a real Clerk tenant token (operator is refused). NOTE (deploy): the new top-level `/v1/account/erase` path must be added to the engine-worker forward list. - fix(serve): **shallow clone (`git clone --depth N`) — handle the TWO-round stateless-HTTP negotiation (the multi-round bug a live clone caught that the single-round hermetic tests missed).** The first shallow deploy advertised `shallow` + handled a `deepen` request, but a real `git clone --depth 1` died `fatal: git fetch-pack: expected shallow list`. A packet trace (`GIT_TRACE_PACKET=1`) showed git does shallow in **two POSTs**: round 1 sends `want + deepen N` with **NO `done`** (the server must reply with the `shallow` boundary section ONLY); round 2 re-sends the boundary as `shallow <oid>` lines **plus `done` and NO `deepen`** (the server must re-send the `shallow` section, then `NAK`, then the pack). The old code detected a shallow request ONLY by `deepen`, so round 2 (no deepen) skipped the required `shallow` section → git aborted. Fix: **hugit-proto** — `parse_client_shallow(body)` extracts the client's `shallow <oid>` lines; `serve_shallow_at(source, wants, boundary)` + `collect_shallow_at` build the depth-bounded pack cutting the commit walk at the CLIENT-declared boundary (round 2) rather than a depth; `ObjectId` re-exported. **hugit-serve (`git.rs`)** — `ClonePlan` gains `shallow_client: Vec<ObjectId>` + `done`, and `is_shallow()` = `deepen` OR client `shallow` lines (both take the shallow path + disable the cached full-clone pack); `build_shallow_pack_bytes` computes the boundary from the depth (round 1 / single-round `deepen`+`done` clients) or the client boundary (round 2), always emits the `shallow` section + flush, and **gates `NAK` + pack on `done`** so a round-1 negotiation returns the boundary only. 3 new hermetic tests that mirror the REAL wire: `parse_client_shallow` extraction; `serve_shallow_at` cuts at the client boundary; round-1-is-negotiation-only (shallow+flush, NO NAK/pack) vs round-2-carries-the-pack (shallow+flush+NAK+pack). Owns `crates/hugit-proto/src/read/{serve/mod.rs,negotiate/mod.rs}` + `crates/hugit-proto/src/lib.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/git.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`). Live-verified end-to-end by a real `git clone --depth 1` post-deploy.
- feat(serve): **an OPTIONAL second operator Bearer (`HUGIT_ENGINE_DEV_TOKEN_EXTRA`) — zero-downtime credential provisioning/rotation without touching the shared primary token.** The engine's operator credential (`HUGIT_ENGINE_DEV_TOKEN`) is a single wrangler secret that a sibling (githugr's site, as `GITHUGR_ENGINE_TOKEN`) also sends to authenticate — and its plaintext is not readable back (write-only secret). Rotating it to a value ops can use would force a matched update on BOTH workers with an unavoidable mismatch window (githugr's reads 401 mid-cutover). This adds an ADDITIVE second operator token: `AppState.dev_token_extra: Option<String>` loaded from `HUGIT_ENGINE_DEV_TOKEN_EXTRA` (absent/whitespace → `None`, no phantom empty token), and `two_tier_auth` matches the Tier-2 dev-token against the primary OR the extra (each via the constant-time `auth::tokens_match`). Setting the EXTRA var provisions a known ops credential with **zero downtime** — the primary is untouched, so the sibling keeps working with no rotation window; it also gives the engine proper add-new→migrate→drop-old rotation going forward. Fail-closed unchanged: `HUGIT_ENGINE_DEV_TOKEN` is still REQUIRED (boot refuses without it); the break-glass gate (`HUGIT_ALLOW_DEV_OPERATOR`) still governs whether a matching Bearer confers operator vs anonymous; a real Clerk session token resolves in Tier 1 and never reaches this branch. NOTE (deploy): a new `HUGIT_*` container var needs BOTH the secret AND an entry in `engine-worker/index.js`'s forward list to reach the container. 1 new hermetic test (`second_operator_token_authenticates_alongside_the_primary`): the extra token authenticates as operator, the primary STILL works untouched, and a Bearer matching neither is a hard 401. - feat(serve): **shallow clone (`git clone --depth N`) — depth-bounded pack serving + the v1 `shallow` section.** A stock git client rejected `git clone --depth 1` against the engine with **"Server does not support shallow clients"**: the v1 `info/refs` advertise never listed the `shallow` capability, so git refused to send `deepen`. This implements real depth-bounded serving (the git-native gap agents/CI hit — `--depth 1` is their default). **hugit-proto:** `parse_deepen(body)` extracts `deepen <N>` from an upload-pack request reusing the tested pkt-line decoder (forward-compatible — unknown lines already ignored; v0 scope is `deepen <N>` only, `deepen-since`/`deepen-not` → a full clone, a valid superset never a corrupt pack); `collect_shallow` is a depth-bounded commit BFS — include commits through depth N, pack EVERY included commit's FULL tree/blob closure (a shallow clone still checks out a complete working tree — only the *ancestry* is bounded), and mark a commit SHALLOW iff at least one parent is NOT in the included set (git-correct across MERGES via a post-walk parent check, and a root commit — no parents — is never shallow); `serve_shallow(source, wants, depth)` + `serve_clone_shallow(view, …)` assemble the depth-bounded pack from the client's VALIDATED wants (`--depth` implies `--single-branch`, so honour the actual wants, not every advertised tip), bounded by the same `SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET` (fail-CLEAN, never a partial closure). **hugit-serve (`git.rs`):** advertise `shallow` in `ADVERTISED_CAPS`; `ClonePlan.shallow_depth` — a `deepen` request forces `full_clone` OFF so the cached full-history clone pack (WP-BC) is NEVER served to a shallow client; `build_shallow_pack_bytes` emits the v1 shallow response — the `shallow <oid>` section (one pkt-line per boundary commit, then a flush that ends the section, emitted even when empty since the client requested a deepen), then `NAK`, then the raw depth-bounded packfile — fail-CLOSED to 404, never a truncated pack (same contract as `build_upload_pack_bytes`). The heavy assembly still runs on the detached upload-pack worker (the accept loop is freed at hand-off, unchanged). 7 new hermetic tests: `parse_deepen` extracts the depth / ignores a normal body / rejects zero+malformed; depth-1 packs only the tip snapshot + marks the tip shallow; depth-2 moves the boundary back (a merge-safe parent check); depth ≥ history == a full clone with NO boundary; depth-0 → 1. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`). Wire-framing end-to-end (the single-round HTTP shallow response) is proven by a live `git clone --depth 1` against the engine post-deploy (staged, not auto-deployed).
- feat(serve): **`POST /v1/repos/{repo}/prs` — open a PR from a pushed branch (head vs base), closing the GitHub-faithful loop (push branch → open PR on its diff → review → land).** Previously a PR could be opened only via `dispatch` (ask an agent) or `edit/{path}/propose` (a web edit) — there was no "open a PR on a branch's diff" verb. New DTO `PrCreateReq { head, base, title, body? }` (`crates/hugit-http-contracts/src/write_requests.rs`) and new pure verb `write_pr_create` (`crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/verbs/write_pr_create.rs`): it resolves `head`/`base` (a short branch/tag, a full `refs/heads/…`, or a raw 40-hex oid) to their tip commit oids against the repo's LIVE refs snapshot — reusing the compare handler's `resolve_refish` (now `pub(crate)`) so the resolution rules match — assigns the next PR number (`count(pr.opened)+1`, the SAME ordinal `dispatch`/`edit-propose` use), and emits ONE `pr.opened` event that PINS both tip SHAs + the branch names + the scrubbed title/body (an unknown head/base → a uniform 404, no existence oracle; a repo with no git seam resolves nothing → 404). Routed through the `with_write` door under the chain-derived `asserted_class` (a write is never anonymous; the door already cleared the ownership gate). **Honesty (no stub PR):** `build_pr_detail` (`crates/hugit-serve/src/handlers/pr_detail.rs`) now reads those pinned SHAs back and renders a REAL head-vs-base numstat via one bounded `diff_commits` (the same primitive `compare` serves), and surfaces the real `source_branch`/`target_branch`/`title` (and the body as `why`) — so a PR read back via `GET /v1/repos/{repo}/prs/{n}` shows the actual diff, not an empty row. A dispatch/edit PR (no pinned SHAs) keeps its existing intent-commit diff path unchanged. Free text (title/body/branch names) is scrubbed at both the write and read boundaries; the pinned 40-hex SHAs are structural. Tests: the full create→`build_pr_detail` round-trip proving a real one-file `+1` diff + correct branches/title, next-ordinal numbering, missing-branch 404 (no write), anonymous/worker principal refused (no write), validation + secret-scrub, and the DTO round-trip.
