- fix(serve): **the boot CAS `batch_read` self-probe is now NON-BLOCKING — the SOTA root fix that makes the `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` 128→256 raise deploy-safe (the sync probe at 256 crash-looped the container → prod outage).** The boot path ran the `batch_read` connectivity self-check (`~18s` at 1024 objects) SYNCHRONOUSLY inside `AppState::from_env` → `load_repos_from_env` (`cas_src.batch_read_selfcheck()`); with `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` raised to 256 that probe grew slow enough to blow the Cloudflare **Container STARTUP** deadline → crash-loop → outage. Fix (`crates/hugit-serve/src/state.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/cas.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs`): the probe no longer runs on the boot path. `AppState.cas_batch_read_health` is now a shared, interior-mutable `Arc<RwLock<String>>` initialized to `"probing"` (a CAS-backed repo loaded) or `"unprobed"` (Local/git-dir mode); `load_repos_from_env` CAPTURES a `crate::cas::CasSelfcheckProbe` for the first CAS repo (a `Send + 'static` handle holding cheap `Arc`-backed clones of the live oid→blake3 index + CAS client — it reads the SAME index the source serves) instead of running the check, and `from_env` SPAWNS a detached `std::thread` (`hugit-cas-selfprobe`, mirroring the existing `spawn_clone_pack_rebuild` detached-thread pattern) that runs the probe and writes the result (`"ok <found>/<req> <ms>ms"` / `"empty-index"` / `"err:…"`) into the shared cell. **Boot returns IMMEDIATELY — never blocked by the probe.** A probe panic is ISOLATED to its thread via `catch_unwind` (records `"err:selfprobe-panicked"`, never crashes boot); a thread-spawn failure records `"err:selfprobe-spawn-failed"` (never a wedged `"probing"`). `/readyz` reads the shared cell (`"probing"` briefly after a deploy, then the landed result) — the `cas_batch_read` field is unchanged in shape. The `batch_read_selfcheck` body was factored into a shared `run_batch_read_selfcheck(&index, &cas)` free fn used by BOTH the synchronous method (kept for direct/test callers) and `CasSelfcheckProbe::run`. Also updated the `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` doc to record that 256 is deadline-safe on the STARTUP path too (not just the request path) now that the probe is off-boot. New hermetic test `selfcheck_probe_runs_off_thread_into_shared_cell` (empty-index short-circuit with no network call; a seeded index → the probe MOVED onto a thread writes `"ok 1/1 …"` into a shared `Arc<RwLock<String>>` — the exact off-boot shape). Completes the trio with the two go-live fixes below (line-scoped README redaction + the `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` 128→256 raise), which are what forced this non-blocking rework. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **two go-live fixes — the home README no longer over-redacts to a bare `[REDACTED]`, and `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` is raised 128 → 256.** **(1) Line-scoped README redaction.** `GET /v1/repos/{repo}/home` returned `readme_html: "[REDACTED]"` for a perfectly normal README: `render_root_readme` scrubbed the WHOLE file through `crate::fmt::scrub` (`hugit_ledger::redact::apply`), which replaces its ENTIRE input with the `[REDACTED]` sentinel the moment ANY span looks secret-shaped — so one secret-shaped line nuked the whole README to prose-less `[REDACTED]` on the web (fail-safe, never a leak, but a real UX bug). Fix: scrub the README **LINE-BY-LINE** (`split('\n')` → `scrub` each line → `join("\n")`, preserving the exact line structure incl. a trailing newline), so ONLY the offending line(s) redact and every other line survives verbatim as raw markdown (githugr still renders + sanitizes it). The read-boundary guarantee is preserved — a real secret in the README STILL redacts (just its line, not the file) — as are the size bound (`MAX_README_BYTES`) and the fail-soft/raw-markdown/no-`<pre>` behavior. New `handlers::home` unit test `secret_line_redacts_only_its_line_normal_lines_survive`: a README with 1 secret-shaped line + several normal markdown lines → the normal lines survive verbatim, only the secret line redacts, the raw secret never appears, and the whole file is NOT a bare sentinel. **(2) `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` 128 → 256 (`cas.rs`).** The 128 was an interim stopgap for a CoreLink batch-read 500 (a SERIAL server-side fan-out tripping the ~21s CF edge deadline at 256×~80ms). **CoreLink #594 parallelized the fan-out (live in prod)**, so 256 is deadline-safe again AND halves the batch-read round-trips (helps the clone-pack background build). The frozen `BATCH_MAX_OBJECTS = 2000` remains the hard per-request cap; this client-sizing stays well under it. No other batch constant changed; the batch-count assertions are computed from the constant (they adapt), only stale `128` comment text updated. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/handlers/home.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/src/cas.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **a freshly-PUSHED repo's files + README are now readable on the web — `build_home`/`build_blob`/`build_edit` resolve the root tree from the LIVE HEAD commit, not the stale `RepoState::git_root_tree` snapshot.** A receive-pack push hot-swaps `git_refs`/`live_oid_index` (so `last_commit`/`branches`/`commit_count` reflect the push) but does NOT refresh the boot-time `git_root_tree`; a freshly-created-then-pushed repo therefore read its EMPTY_TREE snapshot → `files:[]` + `readme_html:""` on the web even though the same objects clone back fine (they ARE in the CAS — the distroless engine has no other source). Fix: a new `live_root_tree(source, head_commit, fallback)` helper reads the HEAD commit → its tree via `hugit_proto::commit_root_tree` (one then-cached read; fail-soft to the boot snapshot when there is no tip or the commit is unresolvable → empty repo stays honest `files:[]`), threaded into the per-request `RepoGit` (root_tree is now OWNED + live-derived, not a `&` to the stale field). hugit itself is unchanged (its live HEAD tree == its ingested `git_root_tree`); a real user's create→push→browse now lights up. Unit test proves the resolver returns the live commit's tree over a differing stale fallback, falls back on no-tip, and fail-softs on an absent commit. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs`.

- feat(serve): **`GET /v1/repos/{repo}/home` now serves the REAL root file TREE + rendered README — the "browse the repo files" table-stakes was a STUB (`files: []`, `readme_html: ""`).** `build_home` now takes the same git content seam (`src: Option<&Arc<dyn ObjectSource>>`, `root_tree: Option<&ObjectId>`) that `build_blob`/`build_edit` already receive, threaded from the SAME `dispatch_repo` call site (`server.rs` `["home"]` arm → `git_source`/`root_tree`). **files:** the DIRECT (one-level) entries of the root tree at HEAD via `hugit_proto::list_tree_at_dir(src, root_tree, "")` (the same primitive the blob sidebar uses; symlinks + gitlinks excluded, capped at `MAX_TREE_ENTRIES`), mapped to `TreeRowVm { name, is_dir, intent_id: None, message: "", age: "" }` and SORTED dirs-first-then-files-alphabetical (the GitHub/githugr file-table order). Per-row last-touch attribution (`intent_id`/`message`/`age`) is honest-empty — a per-entry history walk is a single-thread latency-DoS, a separate seam, never fabricated. **readme_html:** the root README (`README.md` → `README` → `readme.md` → `Readme.md` fallback) resolved via `hugit_proto::resolve_blob_at_path` (path-traversal-guarded); rendering is the WINDOW's job (headless-engine doctrine) — the engine returns the RAW (scrubbed, size-bounded) markdown and githugr renders + SANITIZES it through its single audited `render_markdown` (comrak → ammonia strict-allowlist) sink (one sanitizer, not two; 1/1 mock fidelity). The wire field stays named `readme_html` (non-breaking; a coordinated rename to `readme_raw` is a tracked follow-up) but now carries raw markdown githugr treats as UNTRUSTED — the engine does NO escaping/rendering. **Security (a NEW read surface):** every filename/path AND every README byte is `crate::fmt::scrub`'d at the read boundary (a secret-shaped filename or a secret in the README redacts to the `REDACTED` sentinel — proven by `secret_filename_and_readme_content_redact`); both reads are WALL-CLOCK bounded by a `BudgetedSource`/`WALK_BUDGET` deadline (mirrors `build_blob`/`build_edit` — past the deadline every CAS `get` → `Ok(None)`, so files/readme degrade to honest-empty rather than wedging the single-threaded accept loop; proven by `past_deadline_bounds_home_reads_to_honest_empty` via a testable `build_home_until`); an oversized README (`> MAX_README_BYTES` = 1 MB) is skipped (OOM guard); fail-soft everywhere (missing seam / decode error / deadline → empty files/readme, never a 500/panic). No auth/visibility change (home is already caller-gated). 5 new `handlers::home` unit tests (no-seam empty · dirs-first listing + README render · redaction · deadline bound · raw-markdown-not-escaped) reusing the `edit.rs` in-memory `CasObjectSource` tree doubles; the 21 `parity_home` call sites updated to the additive signature (`None, None`). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/handlers/home.rs` + the `["home"]` arm in `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` + `crates/hugit-serve/tests/parity_home.rs` + this entry; the `RepoHomeVm`/`TreeRowVm` wire shapes are UNCHANGED (fields were already present). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- feat(serve): **WP-BC — WIRE the cached clone pack into the live engine: an anonymous full `git clone` now streams ONE pre-assembled pack from R2 instead of walking the whole object closure per request.** Builds on WP-A's frozen `clone_pack` storage/logic (this WP CALLS it, does not reimplement it). **Serve hook (`git.rs`):** `prepare_upload_pack` computes a strict `full_clone` predicate — `request.haves.is_empty() && want-set == the full advertised tip-set` (empty wants → `clone_all` is a full clone by construction; explicit wants are a full clone iff they EQUAL every advertised tip — set-equality, so a false positive can't serve a wrong pack; a false negative merely slow-walks) — and threads a `ClonePlan { full_clone, refs }` plus the repo's cloned `CloneCacheSeam` through `spawn_upload_pack_worker` → `serve_upload_pack_response`. There, BEFORE the slow `build_upload_pack_bytes` walk, a full clone of a repo WITH a cache calls `clone_pack::try_serve_cached_clone_pack(&seam.r2, &seam.tenant, &seam.repo_slug, &plan.refs)`; a hit emits the response as `pkt_line(b"NAK\n")` (the exact `0008NAK\n` bytes, byte-identical framing to the walk path) followed by the raw cached pack bytes, via the SAME `send`/`respond_bounded` path — and returns. The R2 GET runs on the detached WORKER, never the accept loop. Any miss/stale/GET-error/no-cache FALLS OPEN to the existing walk (never a truncated or wrong pack). **CORRECTNESS: `refset_sha` at BUILD and at SERVE are computed from the SAME live `git_refs` snapshot**, so a streamed pack always matches the advertised refs or it is a cache miss. **Background build (`git.rs::spawn_clone_pack_rebuild`):** a DETACHED, per-repo-guarded (`AppState::clone_pack_building: Arc<Mutex<HashSet<String>>>` — never two concurrent builds of one repo, slot released in ALL paths incl. panic via a drop-guard) thread that runs `build_clone_pack` (the exact `serve_fetch`+`clone_all` assembly) → `store_pack_and_flip` (pack PUT then pointer, atomic flip); any error is logged + dropped (a failed build = no cache = slow fallback). It never blocks the accept loop. **Triggers:** `server.rs::serve_on` bootstraps each CAS repo whose `current.json` is absent/stale at boot (`git.rs::bootstrap_clone_packs`); a CAS-mode push AND delete (post-`ok`) kick a rebuild (the tips moved → the cache is stale). **State (`state.rs`):** new `RepoState.clone_cache: Option<CloneCacheSeam>`, populated ONLY for a CAS repo whose receive-pack write seam is present — it reuses the SAME **write-scoped** `R2Config` the `CasWriteSeam` uses (a build PUTs; a read-only cred 403s), so receive-pack OFF / GIT_DIR mode → `None` → slow-walk fallback (a new `CasWriteSeam::clone_cache_seam` derives it). **Stopgap (`cas.rs`):** `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK` 256 → 128 (CoreLink root-caused the batch-read 500 as a serial fan-out tripping the ~21s edge deadline at 256×~80ms; 128×~80ms≈10.2s is ~2× under — stops the 500; the pack cache is what makes clone fast). **Tests (`tests/git_wire.rs`, +4 over a mock R2 that serves seeded keys + rejects PUTs):** a full clone with a MATCHING cache streams the cached bytes framed `0008NAK\n`+pack; an absent OR stale (`refset_sha` mismatch) `current.json` falls open to the real 7-object walk; a fetch WITH `have`s NEVER serves the cached pack. **API-cleanup (owned per WP-BC):** dropped the vestigial `<R: R2Put+R2PutConditional>` generic on `store_pack_and_flip` (operates on the concrete `seam.r2`). **Deviation:** `build_clone_pack`'s `<S: ObjectSource>` (Sized) generic was widened to `&dyn ObjectSource` so it accepts the engine's `Arc<dyn ObjectSource>` handle (a Sized generic can't take a trait object; `&dyn` is the same type `serve_fetch` itself takes — the in-module smoke test's `&CasObjectSource` still coerces); + two `#[doc(hidden)]` test-support constructors (`R2Config::for_test_endpoint`, `AppState::set_repo_clone_cache`) so the integration test (a separate crate with no `ureq` dev-dep) can point a repo's cache at a mock R2. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).

- feat(serve): **WP-A — the frozen storage + pure-logic layer for a CACHED pre-assembled git clone pack (`crates/hugit-serve/src/clone_pack.rs`, new module).** Anonymous `git clone` of a large repo times out because the full object closure is read from the CoreLink CAS per request (~6862 objects × ~80 ms ≈ 550 s → blows the serve budget → 404). The fix (a LATER WP wires it into the clone door) is to assemble the full-repo pack ONCE, store the raw pack bytes as a SINGLE R2 object, and stream that one object on a full clone. This WP delivers ONLY the storage + logic, hermetically tested — no engine wiring. **Frozen contract (WP-BC codes against these EXACT signatures):** `CloneCacheSeam { r2: R2Config, tenant, repo_slug }`; `CurrentPointer { refset_sha, pack_key, object_count, built_at_ms }` (serde, the `current.json` shape); `clone_pack_current_key(tenant, repo) -> "{tenant}/{repo}/clone-pack/current.json"`; `clone_pack_key(tenant, repo, refset_sha) -> "{tenant}/{repo}/clone-pack/{refset_sha}.pack"`; `refset_sha(refs: &BTreeMap<String,String>) -> String` (the CORRECTNESS GATE — SHA-256 over the SORTED, DEDUPED set of tip OIDs, the map VALUES, joined by `\n`, lowercase hex; ref NAMES are irrelevant to the git closure, so it is deterministic AND order-independent); `load_current<R: R2Get>(...) -> Option<CurrentPointer>` (fail-soft `None` on absent/garbage); `store_pack_and_flip<R: R2Put + R2PutConditional>(seam, refset_sha, pack_bytes, object_count, built_at_ms) -> Result<(), String>` (ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: PUT the pack to `clone_pack_key` FIRST and confirm durable, THEN write `current.json` via the conditional `If-None-Match: *` create; a pre-existing pointer advances via an unconditional PUT — safe because the serve-side gate rejects any stale pointer; `Err` never panics on R2 failure); `build_clone_pack<S: ObjectSource>(source, refs) -> Result<(Vec<u8>, u64), String>` (reuses `RefAdvertisement::from_view` + `WantHave::clone_all` + `serve_fetch` EXACTLY as `git.rs`'s full-clone path — RAW pack bytes, NOT NAK-framed; framing stays in the serve layer); `try_serve_cached_clone_pack<R: R2Get>(r2, tenant, repo, live_refs) -> Option<Vec<u8>>` (the serve gate: `load_current` → `pointer.refset_sha == refset_sha(live_refs)` → GET the pack at `pointer.pack_key`; any mismatch/absence → `None`, caller falls back to the slow walk). `refset_sha` uses the workspace `sha2` crate (no new dep). 14 hermetic tests (in-module `#[cfg(test)]`, over `R2Get`/`R2Put`/`R2PutConditional` doubles): `refset_sha` determinism + order-independence + tip-dedup + moved-tip-changes + lowercase-hex-64; key-helper strings; `CurrentPointer` serde round-trip; `store_pack_and_flip`'s pack-before-pointer write ORDER (via a write-sequence-recording double); `load_current` None-on-absent/garbage + Some-on-valid; `try_serve` returns-pack-on-match / None-on-mismatch / None-on-absent; and a `build_clone_pack` smoke over a tiny 3-object `CasObjectSource` (blob+tree+commit → non-empty pack starting with `PACK`, `object_count == 3`). Additive only: NEW `clone_pack.rs` + one `pub mod clone_pack;` line in `lib.rs`; `git.rs`/`server.rs`/`cas.rs`/`state.rs`/receive paths/authz/siblings UNTOUCHED. Deviation flagged: the frozen `store_pack_and_flip<R>` bound `R` is vestigial (the writes go through the concrete `seam.r2: R2Config`), documented + `#[allow(clippy::extra_unused_type_parameters)]`; because of the concrete `seam.r2`, the order test exercises the flip logic through a double that mirrors the fn body verbatim (the frozen signature is separately compile-checked). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).

- obs(serve): **CAS `batch_read` failures are now OBSERVABLE — surfaces the silent swallow that hid the clone 404 root cause.** Two additive changes, zero behavior change to serving/auth/fallback. **(1) `LazyCasObjectSource::prefetch` error visibility:** the `Err(_) => return` swallow that silently fell back to per-object GETs on any `batch_read` failure now emits `eprintln!("[cas] batch_read failed (batch={}): {e}")` to stderr before returning — so the CAS transport error appears in the engine logs. The fallback behavior (per-object `get` still serves every object) is unchanged. **(2) Boot-time self-check on `/readyz`:** a new `LazyCasObjectSource::batch_read_selfcheck` method picks the first OID from the live oid→blake3 index, issues ONE real `batch_read` call against the CoreLink CAS, and maps the result to a short status string: `"ok"` (bytes present), `"absent"` (batch succeeded but object missing — unexpected), `"empty-index"` (no indexed oids — fresh repo), or `"err:<detail>"` (the failure class we are hunting). At boot, `AppState::from_env` calls this method for the FIRST CAS-backed repo (`load_repos_from_env` — called before wrapping `LazyCasObjectSource` in `Arc`, so the concrete type is in scope) and stores the result in a new `AppState::cas_batch_read_health: String` field (default `"unprobed"` for non-CAS / Local / git-dir boots). The `/readyz` handler now includes a `"cas_batch_read":"<health>"` field in its JSON, so the CAS connectivity state is immediately readable from a `curl /readyz` without needing to inspect stderr logs or trigger a clone. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/cas.rs` (`prefetch` error emit + `batch_read_selfcheck`) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/state.rs` (`AppState::cas_batch_read_health` field + `load_repos_from_env` return type + `from_env`/`new` wiring) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` (`/readyz` JSON field) + this entry; `git.rs`/`authz.rs`/`receive_wire.rs`/handlers/siblings UNTOUCHED. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).

- perf(serve): **`git clone`/fetch of a large repo now assembles in SECONDS via BATCH CAS reads — the O(objects) per-object-GET bottleneck is gone (WP W-CLONE-PERF).** Confirmed live: a `git clone` of hugit (~6862 objects) 404'd at ~300 s. Root cause: the clone's reachability walk (`serve::collect_inner`) AND the pack assembly (`assemble_pack_until`) each fetched EVERY object with a SEPARATE synchronous CAS HTTP GET (~40 ms each) on the single-threaded lazy-git-from-CAS engine → ~2×6862 sequential fetches > the 300 s `SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET` → `DeadlineExceeded` → 404. **Fix — pre-fetch the object closure in BATCHES over the existing FROZEN CAS bulk-read plane (`cas.rs` `batch_read`, `application/x-hugit-cas-batch`, chunked to `BATCH_REQUEST_CHUNK=256`), never a new endpoint:** the `ObjectSource` trait gains a default-no-op `prefetch(&[ObjectId])` that a network-backed source OVERRIDES to bulk-warm its cache; `LazyCasObjectSource::prefetch` batch-reads the not-yet-cached oids in one (internally chunked) call. The reachability walk is now **level-batched BFS** (was DFS): every frontier is `prefetch`ed in one round-trip before it is read, and the full closure is `prefetch`ed once more before assembly (a safety net if a byte-bounded cache evicted an early frontier). So a ~6862-object clone issues **≈29 batch reads (O(objects/256)) instead of ~13 700 single GETs (O(objects))**, and completes far under the (unchanged, generous) 300 s budget. **CONTENT-ADDRESSING IS UNCHANGED — batching alters only the TRANSPORT:** every prefetched object is still fail-closed **double-verified** (BLAKE3 key + git-SHA-1 re-derive) BEFORE it enters the cache, EXACTLY as `get` verifies it; an absent/mislabeled/malformed batch element is simply NOT cached and the later `get` takes the cold path and fail-closes it authoritatively (no poisoned bytes ever served). `serve_fetch`'s SIGNATURE is UNCHANGED (batching is internal), so `git.rs`'s upload-pack call site + the wants-validation + the off-loop worker are UNTOUCHED; the BFS reachable SET is identical to the old DFS (the caller sorts by oid), so the assembled pack is byte-for-byte identical. 3 new hermetic tests (`cas.rs`): a clone-sized (~6862-object) fetch does **ZERO single GETs** and **≤ ceil(objects/256)+3 batch POSTs** (proven with a request-counting transport), the batched lazy path assembles a **byte-identical pack** to the per-object `CasObjectSource`, and a **poisoned object in a batch still fails closed** on `get`. Owns `crates/hugit-proto/src/read/pack/mod.rs` (the `prefetch` trait method) + `crates/hugit-proto/src/read/serve/mod.rs` (level-batched BFS walk + pre-assembly closure prefetch) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/cas.rs` (`LazyCasObjectSource::prefetch` over `batch_read` + the test transport's call counters + tests) + this entry; `git.rs`/`server.rs`/`state.rs`/the handlers/siblings UNTOUCHED. `SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET` kept at 300 s (the perf win is entirely from batching; a batched clone finishes two orders of magnitude under it). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **`git push` (receive-pack) — the gix-pack UNPACK + durable finalize now run on a DETACHED worker that owns the connection; the single accept loop is FREED at hand-off, so a large push never wedges the engine incl. `/readyz` (WP W-RECEIVE-OFFLOOP).** The exact mirror of the just-shipped clone off-loop (FIX-CLONE-OFFLOOP): `git.rs::handle_receive_pack` ran `hugit_proto::write::receive::receive_pack` (bound→unpack→verify→store→anchor) SYNCHRONOUSLY on the single-threaded accept thread, plus the CAS-mode open (an R2 `oid-index.json` read), the durable finalize (objects→CAS + the log compare-and-swap + the conditional If-Match `refs.json`/`oid-index.json` manifest PUTs), and the report-status write — so a large push blocked the loop (and every other request, `/readyz` included) for its whole duration. **Fix (worker-responds-directly):** the accept loop runs ONLY the cheap inline half — the deploy-gate, the pusher auth (`two_tier_auth`), the write-authz (OWNERSHIP), the log load + chain-verify, and the wire parse (framing + single-command shape) — then HANDS a `Send + 'static` `ReceivePlan` to a DETACHED worker (`spawn_receive_pack_worker` → the same `spawn_with_payload` the clone uses) that OWNS the `tiny_http::Request`, opens the write sink, runs the unpack + the mode-specific durable finalize + the in-memory ref hot-swap, and WRITES the report-status itself (`serve_receive_pack_response` → `finish_receive_pack`; the delete path also moved off-loop, so its conditional-manifest R2 writes no longer block the loop either). The loop returns to accept IMMEDIATELY. **`Send + Sync` / hot-swap safety:** `AppState` is `Clone + Send + Sync + 'static`; the plan carries a CLONE of it, but each `RepoState`'s interior-mutable state (`git_refs: LiveRefs(Arc<RwLock<…>>)`, `live_oid_index: LiveOidIndex(Arc<RwLock<…>>)`) is `Arc`-SHARED, so the worker's `apply_cas_push_inmemory` / `apply_cas_delete_inmemory` mutate the SAME maps the loop advertises from — the live ref hot-swap still reflects a pushed/deleted tip with no reboot, exactly as before, only off-loop. The `RwLock` makes the now-possible concurrency (a worker writing while the loop reads, or two push workers) memory-safe; the DURABLE correctness of concurrent same-ref pushes is guarded — as it already was — by the log persist compare-and-swap AND the conditional If-Match manifest PUTs (`conditional_manifest_write`, WP-IFMATCH), NOT by the single-threaded invariant. So the move needs NO `state.rs` change and introduces no race (verified: `AppState`/`ReceivePlan` are `Send` — the worker compiles). **fail-CLOSED unchanged:** `ok` is still emitted ONLY after the durable finalize succeeds (objects→CAS→log→manifests, byte-identical ordering); a rejected/failed push answers a clean per-ref `ng`; a worker panic is isolated by the accept loop's existing `catch_unwind` and drops only that connection; a spawn failure under a push flood SHEDS a fast inline 503 (`respond_push_overloaded` — the `Request` is handed BACK, never unpacked inline, never lost). Every receive-pack security check (bomb caps, the stale-check against the live `git_refs`, the delete-ref default-branch guard, If-Match manifest CAS, no CAS poisoning) is unchanged — only WHERE it runs moved. 1 new hermetic test (`git.rs::offloop_tests::receive_pack_handoff_frees_loop_during_slow_unpack`): a ~400 ms fake unpack runs out-of-band while the hand-off returns in <100 ms with the unpack still unfinished — the accept-loop-not-wedged proof, mirroring the clone's. Rates transcribed VERBATIM from Anthropic's published price list (verified 2026-07-02, <https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing>) for the fleet's model ids — `claude-opus-4-8` ($5/$25/$0.50/$6.25 per MTok input/output/cache-read/cache-write-5m), `claude-sonnet-4-6` ($3/$15/$0.30/$3.75), `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (+alias `claude-haiku-4-5`, $1/$5/$0.10/$1.25), `claude-fable-5` ($10/$50/$1.00/$12.50). `cost_micros` returns `Σ(usage × exact rate)` in integer micro-USD (the downstream `cost_usd_micros` unit) on an EXACT model-id match, `None` for an unknown model (honest-zero — never a default/nearest/fallback rate), and `None` on overflow (checked arithmetic — no panic). Honesty note: rates are stored in **pico-USD per token** (not micro-USD) because Anthropic's cache rates are sub-micro-USD per single token — pico-USD keeps every published rate an EXACT integer with zero approximation. Additive const table; no frozen/serialized type touched, conformance pins unchanged. NEVER a partial/under-counted sum, NEVER an estimate, NEVER the modeled `IntentMetrics` COGS (a partial cost would be UNTRUE for the intent — the #113 per-PR honesty law). Two land paths wired: **(1) `pr land --dispatch`** (`dispatch.rs`) now submits `priced_usage(...).cost_usd_micros` on the runner close instead of the TODO(#64) `None` — the fabric records it verbatim (submit→record→attest); no records / unknown model / overflow → `None` (the fabric keeps its honest-zero derived floor). **(2) the non-dispatch `pr land`** (`capture.rs::capture_on_land`) prices `ctx.usage` into the captured PR envelope's `cost_usd_micros` (its ADR-0001 "derived COGS" meaning — the REAL figure, never the modeled COGS) + the summed cache-split, and stamps `env_manifest = "price_card=<PRICE_CARD_VERSION>"` for reproducibility; GATED fail-safe: only when `full_metrics.is_none()` (never re-price the `--dispatch` runner-authoritative path) AND the manual `--tokens`/`--cost-usd-micros` were left honest-zero (an explicit dogfood figure is NEVER overridden). Absent records / unknown model / overflow ⇒ the capture is byte-identical to today (honest-zero, no stamp). Reproducibility: a rendered cost is `(the log's immutable ctx.usage records) × (the in-repo pinned PRICE_CARD_VERSION)`. **Deviation flagged (envelope on the `--dispatch` path):** the summed usage tokens + price-card version are NOT additionally threaded into the `--dispatch` envelope's `metrics.tokens` — that field carries the fabric's load-bearing §13.1 cache-split readback (the documented `--dispatch` semantic + its test); on that path the real cost rides via the close→fabric-readback and is reproducible from the close body + the log. 11 new hermetic tests (dispatch: exact-Σ golden 20_000_000 µ$ from 2 known-model records, a `pr`-targeted record, unknown-model→whole-honest-zero, no-records→honest-zero; capture: real cost + summed tokens + price stamp in the envelope, unknown-model→honest-zero+no-stamp, manual-flag-not-overridden; `priced_usage` units: no-records→None, overflow→None-not-panic, foreign-target ignored). - feat(cli): **`hugit ctx usage` — the authoring-`/usage` capture verb (WP-COST-2).** The cost-killer prices a landed intent `tokens × exact rate` (Option A) and submits on close, but nothing captured the authoring agent's REAL token spend: dogfood is honest-zero, `--dispatch` submits zeros, and the `--tokens`/`--cost-usd-micros` land flags are hand-typed, not measured. This verb is the CAPTURE seam. `hugit ctx usage --intent <id> --model <model_id> --input N --output N --cache-read N --cache-write N [--model-digest H] [--recorded-at <ms>] [--log <path>]` (or `--pr <id>` as the target alias) appends ONE canonical `ctx.usage` record: `{target_id, target_kind, source:"provider_usage", model, model_digest?, tokens:{input,output,cache_read,cache_write,total}, recorded_at}`, where `total = input+output+cache_read+cache_write` (checked-add, fail-closed on overflow) — the ONLY thing computed. hugit records the provider figures VERBATIM: NO network call, NO pricing (that is WP-COST-1 / land's job). **Append-only ACCUMULATE:** multiple `ctx.usage` for one target are BOTH kept (land SUMS them) — never dedup/overwrite. Absent call ⇒ no record ⇒ land stays honest-zero (never fabricated). **Fail-closed + secret-scrub:** token counts are `u64` (clap rejects negative/non-numeric, exit 2); a missing/ambiguous target is the porcelain error (exit 2, actionable `error.fix`) with NO partial record; the id / model / model-digest are routed through `porcelain::structural_secret_scrub` at the write boundary (a secret-shaped model/id REDACTS, a real slug/digest address SURVIVES) so a credential can never land verbatim on the forever hash-chained log. Appended through the D14 `(Orchestrator, Land)` guard, WC1-locked, atomic-persisted. The `ctx.usage` kind is a CLI/refstore-local constant (`ctx::CTX_USAGE_KIND`) — NOT in `hugit-contracts` (a concurrent WP owns that crate); a future serve mirror reuses it with zero interface change. 10 hermetic unit tests (well-formed record + `total=1700`; two-calls-two-records accumulate; `--pr` alias; missing/ambiguous target → porcelain no-record; token-overflow fail-closed; `log_not_found`; secret-shaped model + id scrubbed; real digest survives). - feat(serve): **a PUBLIC repo is now readable over `/v1` with NO Bearer — the JSON twin of the anonymous `git clone` door (WP W-ANON-V1).** Before: every `GET /v1/repos/{repo}/…` required a Bearer BEFORE any repo work, so a no-Bearer read `401`'d even for a PUBLIC repo — while the git wire already served the same public repo anonymously (`clone_principal` → empty chain → `authorize_read(anon, public)=Allow`). Fix: a new `read_principal` helper (server.rs) mirrors `clone_principal` — it derives the acting principal fail-CLOSED to the ANONYMOUS empty chain (`vec![]`) on a missing/garbage/expired Bearer (NEVER a `401` on the credential, NEVER "authenticated as someone"), then runs the EXISTING `authorize_read` gate: PUBLIC → serve (`200`), PRIVATE/absent → uniform `404` (no existence oracle, unchanged). Applied to the two READ surfaces — the `GET /v1/repos/{repo}/*` read routes and the SSE `/v1/repos/{repo}/events` replay stream. UNCHANGED: the WRITE door (`POST /v1/**` still calls `two_tier_auth` directly → `401` on a bad principal — a write is NEVER anonymous), `POST /v1/repos` provision (no anon-create), `/v1/me/*` (identity-scoped, current behavior kept), `/v1/token`, and the dev-operator break-glass (`read_principal` delegates to `two_tier_auth`, so `HUGIT_ALLOW_DEV_OPERATOR=1` still yields the operator on reads; the ONLY behavior change is credential-FAILURE→anon instead of `401`). The audit/erasure/admin control-plane sub-reads stay `is_operator`-gated (anon fails → `404`), as their existing guard already anticipated. Cache-Control unchanged (all repo reads stay `private, no-store` — fail-closed; a public read is merely a missed cache optimization, never a leak). Tests: anon-public-`200`, anon-private-`404`, garbage-Bearer-public-`200`, garbage-Bearer-private-`404`, foreign-tenant-private-`404`, absent≡private-`404` (no oracle), write-no-Bearer-still-`401`, and the `read_principal` fail-closed-anon derivation (incl. operator-preserved-when-flagged).
- fix(serve): **the clone/fetch is served from a DETACHED worker that owns the connection — the single accept loop is FREED at hand-off, so a real clone COMPLETES and never wedges the engine (WP FIX-CLONE-OFFLOOP).** A CONFIRMED go-live regression: `git clone https://engine.githugr.com/hugit` (now public) ran ~47 s then 404'd on the upload-pack RPC. Root cause: `git.rs` called `hugit_proto::serve_fetch` INLINE on the single accept thread; a real hugit clone (~6862 objects, each a ~7 ms synchronous CAS/R2 fetch on the lazy-CAS engine) takes >45 s, so the proto's `SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET=45 s` deadline fired → `Err(DeadlineExceeded)` → `None` → 404. Raising the budget inline would only re-open the HIGH-severity latency-DoS the audit flagged (a heavy walk wedging the loop, incl. `/readyz`, for its whole duration). A public repo makes that an anon-repeatable DoS. **Fix (worker-responds-directly, modelled on `respond_bounded`'s large-body path):** the accept loop runs ONLY the cheap inline half — `prepare_upload_pack` (read-authz + live ref snapshot, want parse, and **`wants_all_advertised`** validation, which caps the walk ROOT before any worker exists; UNCHANGED) — then HANDS the connection to a DETACHED worker (`spawn_upload_pack_worker` → `spawn_with_payload`) that OWNS the `tiny_http::Request`, assembles the pack (`build_upload_pack_bytes`), and WRITES the reply itself via `send`→`respond_bounded`. The loop returns to accept IMMEDIATELY — it does NOT wait for the ~seconds-long clone, so `/readyz` + other requests stay answerable while the pack builds. `Send + 'static`: the worker owns an `Arc::clone` of the repo's `Send + Sync` `ObjectSource` (concurrent clone workers reading the same CAS are safe — no shared mutable state, no lock across the fetch, no `AppState` on the worker) + the owned `WantHave` + the `Send` `Request`. **DoS bounded WITHOUT wedging:** each worker is capped by the proto's `SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET` (RAISED 45 s → **300 s** — now the SOLE runaway bound, generous enough for a real clone since the loop no longer imposes any shorter cutoff; fail-CLEAN, never a truncated pack); a spawn failure under a clone flood SHEDS a fast inline 503 (`respond_git_overloaded`) — `spawn_with_payload` hands the `Request` BACK on spawn-Err so it is never lost — NEVER a heavy walk inline. So a flood ties up bounded, shed-able WORKER threads while the accept loop stays free. **This CLOSES the earlier Deviation-1** (the interim `run_bounded` shape bounded the wedge at `io_budget` but still blocked the loop in `recv_timeout` for a clone's full duration); server.rs is now UNTOUCHED (the interim `run_bounded` visibility change reverted — the write still rides the existing `pub(crate) respond_bounded`). Scope: upload-pack (clone/fetch) only — the receive-pack (push) unpack is also on-loop + heavy but authed + rarer; FLAGGED as an adjacent follow-up, not fixed here. 4 new hermetic tests (`git.rs::offloop_tests`): a multi-object clone assembles byte-complete (NAK+PACK), an unresolvable want fails closed (no truncated pack), the hand-off frees the caller in <100 ms while a ~400 ms job runs out-of-band on the worker (**the loop-not-blocked proof**), and the payload/connection is delivered intact across the hand-off. Owns `crates/hugit-proto/src/read/serve/mod.rs` (SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET const raise 45→300 s + doc only) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/git.rs` (worker-responds upload-pack + helpers + tests) + this entry; `server.rs`/`state.rs`/`cas.rs`/`merge_hook.rs`/the read handlers/siblings UNTOUCHED. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **two go-live convergence-audit git write/response-path loose ends closed at root — both `git.rs`-centric (WP FIX-GIT-LOOSE-ENDS).** **(A #78, MED-latent — the delete-ref If-Match lost-update race.** FIX-IFMATCH-REMERGE closed the cross-instance same-ref lost-update on the UPDATE path (`commit_cas_push_manifests` re-validates the pusher's `expected` tip on the fresh base on every conditional-PUT attempt), but the SYMMETRIC delete path was left open + explicitly flagged: `finalize_cas_delete` → `remove_cas_ref_from_manifest` re-removed the ref UNCONDITIONALLY on each 412 re-read, never re-checking that the ref still pointed at the `expected` tip the deleter validated. Under `HUGIT_SERVE_ALLOW_MULTI_INSTANCE=1` + >1 instance a delete could clobber a concurrent same-ref UPDATE: the update lands (X→C), the delete's If-Match 412s, re-reads the updated tip C, then blindly deletes it → the update is LOST. **Fix (mirrors the UPDATE fix exactly):** `finalize_cas_delete` / `remove_cas_ref_from_manifest` now take the deleter's `expected: &str` (the value `handle_delete_ref`'s stale-check already validated against the live `git_refs` snapshot, threaded from the receive-pack delete call site as `&cmd.old_oid`), and the refs.json build closure RE-VALIDATES it against the fresh base on EVERY attempt: fresh tip **== `expected`** → remove (a legit delete, incl. the concurrent DIFFERENT-ref merge where only some OTHER ref moved); fresh tip **!= `expected`** (a concurrent same-ref advance) → FAIL CLOSED (`Err`, a non-fast-forward StaleRef, the ref is NOT removed, the update survives); fresh tip **absent** (already deleted) → idempotent no-op; refs.json absent → idempotent no-op. This closes the guard for delete too, so `multi_instance_guard`'s `CONDITIONAL_MANIFEST_PUT_ACTIVE = true` claim is now HONEST for BOTH update AND delete (its doc-comment updated to cite both re-validates). 2 new unit tests: `remove_cas_ref_412_remerge_rejects_concurrent_same_ref_update_no_lost_update` (concurrent stale→C advance vs a delete-expected-B → the delete 412-re-merges, re-validates, FAILS CLOSED — C survives, the ref never removed) + `remove_cas_ref_412_remerge_preserves_legit_delete_on_concurrent_different_ref` (concurrent DIFFERENT-ref advance → the delete still lands, the other ref re-merged). **(B #79, LOW-CF-mitigated — the git response write was not worker-bounded.** FIX-SOCKET-TIMEOUT bounded the `/v1` + SSE response write via `respond_bounded` (a state-free detached-worker wall-clock bound; a body `≤ 16 KiB` stays inline, a larger one is offloaded to a worker so a stalled/zero-window/slow-drain client cannot wedge the single-threaded accept loop), but the git receive-pack/upload-pack RESPONSE write lived in `git.rs` on the plain `request.respond` path — so a client that stops draining a large clone/fetch PACK could still block the loop. **Fix:** `respond_bounded` is now `pub(crate)`; `respond_git` takes the accept-loop's `io_budget` (threaded in exactly like `respond_sse`), and every git response write routes through the shared `send` helper → `crate::server::respond_bounded`. A small git response (both advertises, the report-status, errors, the 404) is `≤ RESPOND_INLINE_MAX` so it stays INLINE (byte-identical to before — a normal clone/report is unaffected); the one large git response (the clone/fetch PACK) is worker-bounded, so a stuck drainer's connection is abandoned at the deadline and the loop is freed. The bounding mechanism itself is proven by the existing `run_bounded`/`respond_bounded` server tests (a slow op past the deadline yields `None` without waiting, freeing the caller); this WP re-uses that path by construction. Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/{cas.rs (delete re-validate), git.rs (thread `expected` into `finalize_cas_delete` + route responses through `respond_bounded`), state.rs (guard doc-honesty only), server.rs (`respond_bounded` visibility only — no logic change)}` + this entry; `merge_hook.rs`/the read handlers/`budgeted_source.rs`/`metrics.rs`/`authz.rs`/siblings UNTOUCHED. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).

- fix(serve): **go-live hardening — two LOW convergence-audit findings closed at root (defense-in-depth, fail-closed).** **(A) Write-side ref-name validation.** `receive_wire::parse_command` accepted any ref beginning with `refs/` — an embedded space/newline/control char, a `..`, or an ~65 KB string all rode through into the CAS `refs.json` JSON key that is re-emitted verbatim into the advertise pkt-lines (an embedded `\n` could smear the framing a client reads). Now every ref name is checked against the subset of git's `check_refname_format(1)` that matters: reject ASCII control chars `0x00–0x1F` + DEL `0x7F` (covers `\n`/`\r`/`\t`/NUL), the git-forbidden set ` ~^:?*[\`, a `..`/`@{`/`//` sequence, a leading/trailing `/`, a trailing `.`, a per-component leading `.` or trailing `.lock`, the bare `@`, and a length cap of 512 — a malformed name fails the push leg cleanly via the new `RecvParseError::BadRefName` (`crates/hugit-serve/src/receive_wire.rs`). Well-formed `refs/heads/*` / `refs/tags/*` are unaffected. **(B) SSRF `is_global_v6` IPv4-embedding gap.** The webhook egress guard returned GLOBAL (allowed) for v6 addresses that embed a private/loopback IPv4: NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96`, 6to4 `2002::/16`, and IPv4-compatible `::a.b.c.d`. `is_global_v6` now extracts the embedded IPv4 from each and re-checks it under the existing v4 non-global rules (so a wrapper of `127.0.0.1`/`10.0.0.0/8`/`169.254.169.254` is refused, while a 6to4 wrapper of a genuinely global v4 stays allowed), and rejects Teredo `2001:0000::/32` outright; the existing rejects and a genuinely global v6 (e.g. `2606:4700::`) are unchanged (`crates/hugit-serve/src/merge_hook.rs`).

- harden(serve/proto): **wall-clock deadline on `git clone`/`fetch` (`serve_fetch`) + `want`-must-be-advertised validation** (convergence-audit HIGH + MED — the last unbounded heavy read on the single-threaded engine). **HIGH — `serve_fetch` had NO wall-clock deadline and no object cap.** Every other heavy read threads a wall-clock budget (`DIFF_BUDGET`, `tree_diff_until`, `BudgetedSource`, `CODE_SCAN_BUDGET`); the clone/fetch reachability DFS (`hugit-proto/src/read/serve/mod.rs::collect_inner`) + pack assembly (`read/pack/mod.rs::assemble_pack`) were the lone miss — a full clone is `2 × #objects` synchronous CAS (R2) fetches on the accept loop, so a large / cold-CAS walk wedges the whole engine (incl `/readyz`) for minutes (the code-search / tree-diff DoS class). Fix: a GENEROUS **`SERVE_FETCH_BUDGET = 45 s`** `Instant` deadline threaded through `serve_clone`/`serve_fetch` → `serve_request` → `collect_inner` (checked once per DFS iteration = per CAS get) AND `assemble_pack_until` (checked per object). **Crucially FAIL-CLEAN, NOT truncate:** a clone/fetch pack missing a reachable object is a CORRUPT clone, so exceeding the deadline ABORTS the whole fetch with `ServeError::DeadlineExceeded` / `PackError::Deadline` (the `upload_pack` handler maps that to no-pack, never a half-written pack) — a legit clone under budget is byte-identical + unaffected, only a runaway is bounded. New public `serve_fetch_until(deadline)` (mirrors `tree_diff_until`) for a deterministic past-deadline test; existing `serve_clone`/`serve_fetch`/`assemble_pack` signatures UNCHANGED (wrappers compute the budget) so no caller ripples. **MED — `upload_pack` did not validate `wants` against advertised refs** (`hugit-serve/src/git.rs`): a client `want <oid>` was passed straight to `serve_fetch` with no check it is an advertised/reachable tip. Fix: new `wants_all_advertised(refs, wants)` — each explicit `want` MUST be an advertised ref tip for THIS principal (the same `refs` the advertise exposed, already read-authz-gated), else the request is rejected → **404 no-oracle**; the `uploadpack.allowReachableSHA1InWant`-OFF default. This also caps the HIGH (a caller cannot force the walk from an arbitrary interior root — only a real tip's closure). The full-clone (empty-wants) path derives its wants from `refs` by construction, so it is inherently advertised. Tests: `serve::budget_tests` (a small fetch/clone under budget completes with the full closure; a PAST-deadline fetch → `Err(DeadlineExceeded)`, never a partial pack) + `git::want_validation_tests` (advertised tip accepted; non-advertised / mixed rejected; empty vacuously ok). Owns only `read/serve/mod.rs` + `read/pack/mod.rs` (assemble deadline) + `lib.rs` (re-export) + `git.rs` (thread + wants check) + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- harden(serve): **per-tenant repo cap on `POST /v1/repos` — closes an authenticated create-spam memory-leak + R2-bloat DoS** (WP FIX-PROVISION-CAP — a HIGH convergence-audit finding flagged by two auditors). Every successful provision permanently leaks a `&'static RepoState` (`state.rs::insert_runtime_repo` `Box::leak`, never freed) AND writes a durable genesis object, and NOTHING capped repos-per-tenant — so any authenticated Clerk tenant could loop `POST /v1/repos {name:a1}, {name:a2}, …` and OOM the single-instance, single-threaded engine → a full outage. The `repos_runtime` doc claimed the leak was "bounded by rare human-driven provisions" but nothing ENFORCED that. **Fix:** a new `pub const MAX_REPOS_PER_TENANT: usize = 100` (generous for a real user/org, hard-bounds the DoS) + a new `AppState::count_owned_repos(&self, owner_tenant) -> Option<usize>` that counts the tenant's repos across the authoritative in-memory set (the boot `repos` set ∪ the runtime overlay) filtered by `owner_tenant` — CHEAP (bounded by the loaded-repo count, each candidate log loaded exactly once; it NEVER enumerates the durable/R2 store, which would itself be a DoS). The provision path (`write_provision::provision`) now enforces the cap right after deriving `owner_tenant` and BEFORE the no-clobber pre-check, the genesis persist, and the `&'static` leak: at/over the cap → **429 `TOO_MANY_REPOS`** with NO durable write and NO seam leak. **Fail-closed:** `count_owned_repos` returns `None` if ANY candidate log can't load/verify (an unloadable candidate is genuinely ambiguous re: ownership), and the verb maps `None` → **503** refusal — a transient read fault can never be leveraged to slip past the cap. The cap is PER-TENANT (scoped by the caller-derived `owner_tenant`, never a global create ceiling). Owns only `crates/hugit-serve/src/{state.rs, writes/verbs/write_provision.rs}` + this entry; `server.rs`/`cas.rs`/`git.rs`/the read handlers/siblings UNTOUCHED (the check lives entirely in the verb — no route hook needed). 5 new tests (`write_provision::{under_cap_creates_normally, at_cap_refuses_429_with_no_leak_and_no_write, cap_is_per_tenant_not_global}` + `state::{count_owned_repos_filters_by_owner_tenant, count_owned_repos_fails_closed_on_unloadable_candidate}`). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- harden(serve): **inbound socket read/write deadline on the single-threaded `tiny_http` accept loop** (WP FIX-SOCKET-TIMEOUT — go-live availability). Closes a slow-loris / slow-drain wedge: the body read (`read_body_capped` → `read_to_end`) and the response write (`request.respond`) run SERIALLY on the one accept-loop thread, the body read BEFORE auth and BEFORE the `catch_unwind` isolation. A client that dribbles a POST body (1 byte/min) or stalls the TCP window on a large response would block that thread INDEFINITELY → starve `/readyz` + every other request → outage (the `ShedGate` can't help — it observes between-request saturation, not a hang INSIDE one read/respond). **tiny_http 0.12 exposes NO socket timeout** — verified: `ServerConfig` carries only `addr`+`ssl` (no per-connection config) and `Request` never surfaces the `TcpStream` (`as_reader`/`into_writer`/`remote_addr` only), so neither an OS `SO_RCVTIMEO`/`SO_SNDTIMEO` nor a listener-level timeout is reachable — the clean socket-timeout fix is genuinely unavailable in this version. **Best available bound (implemented in `server.rs` ONLY):** a wall-clock deadline via a detached worker thread. `run_bounded(budget, f)` runs a potentially-socket-blocking op on a worker and waits at most `budget`; on completion → `Some(v)` (worker joins, no leak); on timeout / worker-panic / spawn-failure → `None` and the accept loop is FREED (returns to accept) while the stuck worker is ABANDONED — it parks on its blocking syscall until the OS unblocks it, then drops the `Request` it owns, closing the peer. **(1)** the POST body read is bounded (`read_body_bounded` → drop the connection + `continue` on timeout — the primary, pre-auth, attacker-controlled vector); **(2)** the response write is bounded (`respond_bounded`): a body `<= 16 KiB` is written INLINE (it fits the kernel send buffer, cannot block — `/readyz`, `/metrics`, ordinary JSON keep their exact pre-change fast path), a LARGER body (blob/search/SSE replay) is offloaded to a worker so a zero-window/slow-drain reader cannot wedge the loop; the loop waits at most `budget` so handler execution stays SERIAL (the documented single-thread invariant is preserved — the only concurrency is a state-free abandoned socket write). **Deadline** default `60 s`, env-tunable `HUGIT_SERVE_IO_DEADLINE_SECS`, CLAMPED to `[5, 3600]` s so a mis-set env can neither false-drop a legit slow request (floor) nor un-bound the wedge (ceiling). 60 s cannot false-drop a legitimate client: the 8 MiB body cap clears 60 s at just ~140 KB/s, and the deployed engine sits behind an edge (Cloudflare) that buffers the client body + drains the response over a fast backbone, so the origin sees I/O at backbone speed — while a 1-byte/60 s slow-loris is caught long before it could ever finish a body. A timeout can NEVER crash the loop (a worker panic disconnects the channel → `recv_timeout` `Err` → `None`; the loop never panics). 5 new hermetic tests on the load-bearing primitive: `run_bounded` returns a fast value / moves a non-`Copy` value through intact / a 5 s op under a 100 ms budget returns `None` in <2 s (proves the caller is freed at the deadline, not wedged for the op's duration — the slow-loris outcome) / a worker panic is `None` not a crash / the deadline clamp (unset→60, floor 5, ceiling 3600). **Flagged residuals (fail-closed, tracked):** (a) a timed-out worker parks ONE thread per malicious connection until its syscall unblocks — a bounded cost, NOT an accept-loop wedge (and a spawn-failure under that flood degrades to a shed `None`, never an inline block); (b) the git receive-pack/upload-pack RESPONSE write happens inside `git.rs` (out of this WP's owned scope) so it is not yet worker-bounded — but the git POST BODY read IS bounded (it flows through `read_body_bounded` in `server.rs` before dispatch); (c) the bound is a total-operation wall-clock deadline, not a per-read idle timeout (which would need the unreachable `SO_RCVTIMEO`) — a generous deadline cleanly separates legit (completes fast) from malicious (never completes). Owns only `crates/hugit-serve/src/server.rs` + this entry. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **the If-Match manifest re-merge now RE-VALIDATES the pusher's `expected` ref precondition against the fresh base — closing a cross-instance same-ref lost-update the `multi_instance_guard` falsely green-lit** (WP FIX-IFMATCH-REMERGE, a MED go-live correctness finding). The authoritative receive-pack stale-check runs against a PER-INSTANCE in-memory `git_refs` snapshot; the durable refs.json write is a conditional (If-Match) compare-and-swap. On a **412** the re-merge re-read the fresh base and re-applied `refs.insert(ref, new_oid)` **UNCONDITIONALLY** — it never re-checked the pusher's `expected` tip. With `HUGIT_SERVE_ALLOW_MULTI_INSTANCE=1` + >1 instances this was a silent lost update: A (X→A, expected=X) and B (X→B, expected=X) each pass their LOCAL stale-check; A commits refs=A; B's If-Match 412s, re-reads refs=A, then BLINDLY overwrote to B — a non-fast-forward that MUST be StaleRef was silently accepted and A's update LOST. Since `multi_instance_guard` permits >1 instances whenever `CONDITIONAL_MANIFEST_PUT_ACTIVE` (a `const true`), the guard claimed a safety the code did not provide (If-Match was necessary but NOT sufficient). **Fix:** `commit_cas_push_manifests`/`finalize_cas_push` take the pusher's per-ref `expected: Option<&str>` (`None` for a create — the SAME value the stale-check validated, threaded from the receive-pack call site as `req.update.expected`), and the refs.json build closure RE-VALIDATES it against the fresh base on EVERY attempt (first PUT and every 412 re-read): if the fresh tip for the ref no longer equals `expected` it is a genuine non-fast-forward → the closure returns `Err` (StaleRef) and the ref is **NOT** advanced (fail-closed via `CasPushError::Manifest`, the existing per-ref `ng` path — no lost update, no unconditional overwrite). Only when the fresh base still shows `expected` (a create whose ref is still absent, or an update whose tip is unchanged — INCLUDING the legitimate concurrent-DIFFERENT-ref merge where only some OTHER ref moved) is the tip re-applied. This makes the If-Match path actually **sufficient**, so the `CONDITIONAL_MANIFEST_PUT_ACTIVE = true` guard is now HONEST (its doc-comment cites this fix as what makes it sound; retry bounds + fail-closed-on-exhaustion unchanged). 2 new unit tests: `commit_cas_push_manifests_412_remerge_rejects_stale_expected_no_lost_update` (concurrent same-ref advance X→A → our X→B 412-re-merges, re-validates, FAILS CLOSED — A survives, B never written) and `commit_cas_push_manifests_412_remerge_preserves_legit_concurrent_different_ref` (concurrent DIFFERENT-ref advance → our update still lands, the other ref re-merged). Owns `crates/hugit-serve/src/cas.rs` (the re-merge closure + finalize path) + `crates/hugit-serve/src/state.rs` (the guard-honesty doc only) + a MINIMAL `crates/hugit-serve/src/git.rs` change (the receive-pack finalize call site passes `expected`) + this entry. **Flagged:** (1) the `finalize` call in `git.rs` is the one permitted call-site edit (threading `expected`); the client-facing `ng` reason stays the generic `manifest-write-failed` (the handler's `CasPushError::Manifest` arm is untouched per the no-handler-edits constraint — the semantic guarantee, ref-not-advanced + no lost update, holds; a distinct `non-fast-forward` wire reason is a follow-up once handler edits are in scope). (2) The SYMMETRIC delete-ref path (`finalize_cas_delete`/`remove_cas_ref_from_manifest`) has the same class of race (a concurrent same-ref advance vs a delete-expected), but threading `expected` there requires editing `handle_delete_ref` (a handler, out of this WP's constraint) — flagged as an adjacent follow-up, NOT fixed here. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).

The multi-tenant go-live's self-service seam. A new write verb `crates/hugit-serve/src/writes/verbs/write_provision.rs` (registered in `writes/verbs/mod.rs`; routed in `server.rs` under `["v1","repos"]`, BEFORE the `{repo}/tail` write dispatch). **Auth + ownership:** the SAME two-tier Bearer gate, then the verb derives `owner_tenant` from the caller's `clerk:{org}:{user}` principal — NEVER a request field, so a body can never spoof ownership; the platform operator (`orchestrator:*`) and an anonymous caller are **401** (no god-create / anon-create over the public door — a real user creates their OWN repo). **Body** `{ "name", "visibility"? }`: `name` is `[a-z0-9._-]`, 1..=64, no path separator, no leading dot, no `..` (a stricter subset of `is_safe_repo_slug`) → **400** otherwise; `visibility` defaults `"private"`, must be `"public"`/`"private"` → **400** otherwise. **The one atomic op (fail-closed, lands-or-nothing):** the durable artifact is the genesis `<repo>.json` event-log with a single chain-anchored `repo.meta{owner_tenant, visibility}` record (seq 0), persisted via ONE **create-only** compare-and-swap (`CasToken::Absent` → Local content-compare / R2 `If-None-Match: *`). (3) The push/clone-live legs need real CAS and are proven at the mechanism level hermetically (runtime insert visible to `repo_state`; genesis create/read/authz/409/401/400/no-clobber e2e in Local mode); the full push→clone round-trip against prod CAS is a live-smoke item (same posture as the rest of the CAS write path). Closes a real single-thread latency-DoS on the go-live read path: on the single-threaded lazy-git-from-CAS engine each tree/blob is a synchronous R2 fetch that blocks the whole accept loop, and TWO read handlers ran N tree-diffs each with its OWN fresh `hugit_proto::DIFF_BUDGET` and NO shared cap — so a crafted PR bundle wedged the engine for `N × 2 s` (minutes). **`handlers/pr_detail.rs`**: `build_pr_detail` now establishes ONE `diff_deadline = now + DIFF_BUDGET` and threads it through BOTH `build_intents` (the N per-intent first-parent diffs) AND `pr_level_diff` (the tip diff) via a new local `diff_first_parent_until` that walks with `hugit_proto::tree_diff_until(deadline)` instead of a per-call budget — so the WHOLE PR render's diff work costs ~ONE `DIFF_BUDGET`, not `N ×`. **`handlers/review.rs`**: `pr_diffstat` now creates ONE shared deadline and threads it into every `intent_commit_diff` (via `tree_diff_until`), so the ≤64-intent diffstat is bounded by one budget, not `64 × DIFF_BUDGET ≈ 128 s`; `MAX_REVIEW_DIFF_INTENTS` stays as a cheap defense-in-depth RESULT cap (no longer the load-bearing latency guard). **`handlers/blob.rs`**: mirrors `handlers/edit.rs` — `build_blob` now wraps the source in `crate::budgeted_source::BudgetedSource` (`WALK_BUDGET = 2 s`) for the `resolve_blob_at_path` per-segment CAS walk AND the sidebar-dir + history walks (all per-segment CAS walks share the render's ONE deadline); split out `build_blob_until(deadline)` for a deterministic bound test. **Honest-partial, never fabricated:** past the shared deadline `tree_diff_until` returns its partial (typically empty) diff and the blob resolve returns `None` → 404 — the same honest-partial the per-diff `tree_diff` already applies at its own budget (the `DiffVm` wire carries no truncation flag, so there is no oracle either way; consistent with the existing truncation semantics). REUSES the existing `hugit_proto::tree_diff_until` + `crate::budgeted_source::BudgetedSource` (no new budget primitive). Owns only `handlers/{pr_detail,review,blob}.rs` + this entry; no change to `diff.rs`/`server.rs`/`state.rs`/the contract crate. - feat(serve): **graceful load-shed + `/metrics` observability on the single-threaded accept loop** (WP W-SHED-METRICS — go-live production-readiness). The deployed engine is a SINGLE-THREADED synchronous `tiny_http` accept loop that processes requests SERIALLY; under a sustained burst, arrivals stack behind slow work. New module `crates/hugit-serve/src/metrics.rs`. **Part A — fail-safe load-shed (`ShedGate`):** the ONLY shed signal is CONTINUOUS accept-loop saturation — the loop has been busy back-to-back, never idle, for longer than `HUGIT_SHED_SATURATION_MS` (default `5000`; `0` = OFF). It is fail-safe BY CONSTRUCTION: any idle gap (`waited_ms > HUGIT_SHED_IDLE_RESET_MS`, default `5` — the queue drained, the engine is keeping up) RESETS the run, so normal load AND a bursty-but-serviceable burst the loop clears within the window are served IN FULL — never a false 503. A request that OPENS a saturation run has `sat_run == 0` and is therefore never the one shed. Only a request arriving into a loop that has demonstrably been unable to drain for seconds is shed (fast `503` + `Retry-After: 1`). The decision is pure saturating arithmetic (CANNOT panic) AND is additionally wrapped in `catch_unwind` that degrades to SERVE on any hypothetical panic — so the guard can neither false-503 nor crash the availability-critical loop. `/readyz` (liveness) + `/metrics` (observability) are shed-EXEMPT so they stay answerable precisely during an overload. **Part B — `/metrics` (compact JSON, unauthenticated, NO tenant data):** `GET /metrics` serves aggregate counters — `in_flight` (0/1 on this loop), `requests_total`, `shed_total`, a rolling-window `latency_ms.{p50,p99}` (bounded 1024-sample ring), and `per_route` counts. Route labels are a FIXED CLOSED vocabulary of route CLASSES (`readyz`/`repo_read`/`git`/…), never a concrete path/slug/principal/id — so the endpoint leaks nothing. `cache_occupancy` is reported `null` (the decoded-object cache lives behind `git.rs`/`state.rs`, not owned by this WP + no occupancy accessor exists — honestly unavailable, not faked; a tracked additive follow-up). Plus a per-request structured stderr log line: `req id=<monotonic> route=<class> status=<code> duration_ms=<n>` (`status=0` = a sub-handler owned its own status, e.g. SSE/git). Cheap on the single thread (atomics + one uncontended lock; no added per-request latency of note). Touches ONLY `server.rs` (loop wiring + the two new routes) + the new `metrics.rs` (+ `lib.rs` `pub mod`). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- harden(serve): **wall-clock DoS bound on the `edit`/blob path→blob CAS resolve + a reusable `BudgetedSource` guard** (WP W-BUDGET-AUDIT — single-thread go-live hardening). The deployed engine is single-threaded lazy-git-from-CAS: every `ObjectSource::get` is a synchronous R2 fetch that blocks the whole accept loop (incl `/readyz`), so a read whose CAS-fetch count scales with an attacker input is a latency DoS. New module `crates/hugit-serve/src/budgeted_source.rs` — **`BudgetedSource`**, a wall-clock-deadline wrapper over any `ObjectSource`: once the shared deadline passes every further `get` returns `Ok(None)` (honest "not resolved within budget" — a walk built on it STOPS and yields its honest-empty result, NEVER a fabricated object, NEVER an unbounded loop; the deadline is checked BEFORE each fetch so no over-budget R2 read even starts). `tripped()` lets a caller observe a budget-cutoff vs a real miss (both honest — no existence oracle). `WALK_BUDGET = 2 s`, in lock-step with `hugit_proto::DIFF_BUDGET` / `BLOB_HISTORY_BUDGET`. **Applied in `handlers/edit.rs`**: `resolve_blob_at_path` does one CAS `get` per path segment (count-capped at `MAX_PATH_DEPTH=64`, but that count-cap is NOT a latency cap under a cold cache / slow R2 / a deep ingested tree), so `build_edit` now wraps the source in `BudgetedSource` — past the deadline the resolve returns an honest `None` → 404. Split out `build_edit_until(deadline)` for a deterministic bound test (a present file is honest-404'd once the budget is spent; the far-future case resolves normally). **Audit of the remaining CAS-walk read handlers (grounded, per-handler — no blanket claim):** `commit_detail` runs exactly ONE `diff_against_first_parent` → already wall-clock-bounded by `DIFF_BUDGET` (W-DIFFVM); `landing`/`releases`/`attention`/`insights`/`campaign` take NO git source — they iterate the already-loaded in-memory `EventLog` (count-capped output, no per-record R2 fetch → not a CAS-walk DoS); `search`/`blob_history` are already wall-clock-bounded (`CODE_SCAN_BUDGET` / `BLOB_HISTORY_BUDGET`, verified). **FLAGGED (out of this WP's owned scope, real finding):** `handlers/pr_detail.rs::build_intents` runs `diff_against_first_parent` per bundle intent with a FRESH `DIFF_BUDGET` each and NO cap on the bundle size (N × 2 s), and `handlers/review.rs::pr_diffstat` runs up to `MAX_REVIEW_DIFF_INTENTS=64` diffs each with its own `DIFF_BUDGET` (64 × 2 s ≈ 128 s worst case) — both should share ONE deadline via the existing `hugit_proto::tree_diff_until` instead of one budget per diff; deferred to a follow-up that owns those files. Owns only: `budgeted_source.rs` (new) + `lib.rs` (`pub mod`) + `handlers/edit.rs`. Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **harden the one deployed security boundary for go-live — `Cache-Control: private, no-store` on authed/private responses + an env-gated dev-token→operator god-path (default OFF)** (WP W-CACHE-GODPATH). **Part A (cache):** every AUTHENTICATED `/v1` response now carries `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, and it is added UNCONDITIONALLY to the private-repo SSE event stream (githugr seam #18 — a private VM must never be cached by an intermediary CDN/browser/proxy). Classification is by route and FAIL-CLOSED: a response is left cacheable ONLY when it is explicitly one of the two PUBLIC pre-auth routes (`GET /readyz`, `GET /v1/me/login`); everything else (all `/v1/repos/*` + `/v1/me/*` reads, the SSE stream + its error envelopes, the `POST /v1/token` session-token issuance, every write verb, unknown paths, and auth-failure responses) is private — so a PUBLIC anonymous read stays cacheable while nothing gated on a caller's identity ever is. **Part B (god-path gate):** the dev-token → operator (`orchestrator:hugit`, sees-all) derivation in `two_tier_auth` (Tier-2) is now gated behind a new `AppState::allow_dev_operator` flag, read once at boot from `HUGIT_ALLOW_DEV_OPERATOR` (strict `== "1"`, fail-closed on any other value). **Default OFF** — **the PUBLIC prod deploy OMITS the flag → the public door has ZERO operator god-path**: a Bearer matching the dev-token degrades to an ANONYMOUS principal (empty chain — public reads only, nothing private/operator-gated; the write-door re-checks `authorize_write`, which denies an anonymous chain), never operator. Turning the flag ON (`=1`) is a documented ops/bootstrap **break-glass** that preserves the historical dev-token → operator behavior. **GO-LIVE INVARIANT (non-negotiable, tested both flag states):** a real Clerk-minted session token is resolved in Tier-1 (`clerk:{org}:{user}`) and can NEVER reach the Tier-2 dev-token branch — a real user is STRUCTURALLY incapable of becoming the operator, flag or no flag. The explicit non-env dev/test constructor `AppState::new` defaults the break-glass ON (it is a dev/seed constructor; production boots via `from_env`, which is default-OFF), so existing hermetic tests + the in-repo `scripts/write-smoke.sh` (dev-token path) stay green with no churn; a test asserting the no-god-path behavior flips the pub field to `false`. **Deploy sequencing note:** the public engine drops the flag ONLY after the githugr www + the write-smoke move their render-verify/probe to a real Clerk-minted token — the LEAD's coordination, out of this WP. 7 hermetic tests: cache-control route classification (public cacheable vs authed/unknown/SSE private + the exact `private, no-store` value); break-glass ON → operator, OFF → anonymous-never-operator, garbage bearer → 401 both states, and the Clerk-token-never-operator invariant with the flag ON and OFF. Owns only `server.rs` (two_tier_auth + response headers), `state.rs` (the boot flag), `scripts/write-smoke.sh` (doc note), `CHANGELOG.md`; `authz.rs` unchanged (the derivation, not the classification, is gated). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- fix(serve): **`/v1/me/*` + `/v1/orgs/{name}` scoped to the CALLER — closes a cross-principal READ exposure** (WP W-METENANT, the multi-tenant go-live's githugr NEED #1). Before, every `/v1/me/dashboard`, `/v1/me/attention`, and `/v1/orgs/{name}` route authenticated the caller and then rendered a HARDCODED default repo (`ME_DEFAULT_REPO = "hugit"`) as "your repositories" — the builders took no principal, so any valid session saw the launch repo's operational data. Real cross-principal leak. Now a new **per-tenant repo index** on `AppState` — `me_repo_logs(principal) -> Vec<(slug, verified-log)>` (+ its slug projection `repos_for`) — enumerates the loaded repos and includes each one IFF the caller may `authorize_read` it, **REUSING the exact same read-authz predicate the per-repo gate runs** (no second visibility gate to drift): an **operator** (`orchestrator:*`) sees all loaded repos; a **tenant** (`clerk:{org}:{user}`) sees its own private repos + any public repo; **anonymous / unknown / malformed** principals → EMPTY (identity-scoped: no tenant ⇒ no "my repos", never all-public, never the default). The me/* + org routes now pass the caller's real repo set to three new aggregating builders — `build_me_dashboard` / `build_me_attention` / `build_me_org` — which fold each caller repo's activity ACROSS the set (one repo row per repo; inbox merged + bounded by `PR_CARDS_CAP`; `attention_count` summed; the attention feed concatenated then triage-sorted globally; org rows aggregated under the path-param header). **`ME_DEFAULT_REPO` is DELETED** from the me/* path. **VM WIRE SHAPE byte-unchanged** (`DashboardVm`/`AttentionVm`/`OrgVm` reuse the per-repo builders verbatim; only the DATA becomes the caller's own repos) — a caller who owns no loaded repo gets an honest EMPTY view (`repos: []`), never a 404-vs-200 oracle and never the default repo's data. **No oracle:** a foreign tenant's private repo is simply absent from the index (the caller cannot tell it exists). **Fail-closed:** a repo whose log can't load/verify is EXCLUDED (never included by default); each candidate log is loaded EXACTLY ONCE (single-thread engine — no double-fetch), bounded by the loaded-repo count. Tests: the core two-tenant disjoint-private isolation at both the `AppState` index layer AND end-to-end through the real route+auth; anonymous/unknown/malformed → empty; operator → all; public + own-private for a tenant; unloadable-repo exclusion; cross-aggregate bounds; per-VM round-trip (wire shape) + empty-honest defaults. Owns only `server.rs` (me/* routes), `handlers/{dashboard,attention,org}.rs`, `state.rs`; reuses `authz.rs` unchanged. - fix(serve): **conditional (If-Match) manifest PUT on the receive-pack finalize — the lost-update race is now CLOSED, the HARD pre-condition for `max_instances>1`** (WP-IFMATCH). Before, the receive-pack finalize rewrote the mutable `refs.json` + `oid-index.json` manifests via an UNCONDITIONAL `put_object` (`commit_cas_push_manifests`/`remove_cas_ref_from_manifest`), so two engine instances rewriting a manifest in the read→PUT gap would last-write-wins clobber a concurrent push's ref tip — safe ONLY by the `max_instances:1` single-writer invariant (documented, not mechanized). Now every manifest write is a store-side compare-and-swap: **(1)** two new seam traits — `R2GetVersioned` (fetch bytes + the R2 ETag as a `CasToken`) and `R2PutConditional` (`put_object_conditional(key, body, expected) -> Result<new_etag, ManifestPutError>`, sending `If-Match: <etag>` or `If-None-Match: *` for a create, surfacing the store's **412** as the distinct, retryable `ManifestPutError::Precondition`) — implemented for `R2Config` by REUSING the existing SigV4 signer + the `etag`-header capture (new `R2Config::get_object_etag` + `conditional_object_put`, mirroring the event-log `put_conditional`). **(2)** A bounded `conditional_manifest_write` helper threads the read→build→conditional-PUT loop: a 412 RE-READS the fresh base and RE-APPLIES the ref delta (a MERGE — only OUR ref tip / OUR oid additions are set, so a concurrent push's advance survives; re-merge, never clobber), capped at `MANIFEST_CAS_MAX_ATTEMPTS`; exhaustion FAILS CLOSED with an `Err` (the push stays durable in CAS, the prior manifest keeps serving, the ref is NOT advanced on a poisoned base) — NEVER an unconditional fallback PUT. **(3)** A fail-closed multi-instance boot guard (`state::multi_instance_guard`, wired into `AppState::from_env`): since the Cloudflare instance count is not knowable in-process, an operator declaring `max_instances>1` MUST set `HUGIT_SERVE_ALLOW_MULTI_INSTANCE`; the guard refuses to boot if multi-instance is declared with the write path enabled but the conditional manifest-PUT path is not active — mechanizing the single-writer invariant. **SECURITY (fail-closed):** a present manifest whose GET returns NO ETag (`CasToken::Unsupported`) is REFUSED (no silent degrade to an unconditional PUT that would re-open the race); the conditional PUT refuses an `Unsupported` expected token before any network call. 11 new hermetic tests: MapR2 conditional semantics (create/If-None-Match · update/If-Match hit · stale-412 · double-create-412), commit creates-on-empty-R2, the **lost-update race closed** (a concurrent instance advances refs.json → stale-ETag 412 → re-read + re-merge → BOTH refs survive), retry-exhaustion fails-closed with no manifest advanced, fail-closed on a missing ETag (the PUT is guarded to prove it never runs), delete-absent is a no-op with no fabrication, conditional delete end-to-end, the 4-case boot guard, and the `Unsupported`-token PUT refusal. Owns only `cas.rs` + `state.rs`; no handler/`git.rs` change (the finalize call sites compile unchanged — `R2Config` satisfies the new bounds). Gate green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- feat(serve): **KungFu merge-event webhook — signed, SSRF-safe outbound egress on the live push finalize** (WP W-WEBHOOK, pilot event #72). On a SUCCESSFUL `git-receive-pack` finalize (create / update / delete, both GIT_DIR and CAS modes) the engine now emits a signed `KungFuMergeEvent { repo, ref, base_sha, head_sha, touched_paths[], landed_at, actor, cv_hint }` (frozen shape) to a configured KungFu webhook. New `crates/hugit-serve/src/merge_hook.rs` owns the whole egress; `git.rs` gains only the minimal post-durable call site. **Never regresses the push:** `emit_merge_event` fires AFTER `finalize_cas_push`/`finalize_cas_delete` returned `Ok` AND the `report-status` `ok` was already written to the client — a hook fault (bad config, full queue, hanging endpoint, SSRF refusal) can never change the push result. **Off the accept loop:** the network egress runs on a dedicated worker thread draining a BOUNDED (1024) in-memory queue; enqueue is non-blocking (a full queue drops-oldest + logs a running counter, never blocks); at-least-once delivery with bounded retry/backoff; idempotency key `(repo, head_sha)` on the `X-Hugit-Idempotency-Key` header (an in-flight duplicate is collapsed). **`touched_paths`** is a real base→head tree-diff (`hugit_proto::tree_diff_until`) WALL-CLOCK bounded (1.5 s, below `DIFF_BUDGET`) and row-capped (`MAX_DIFF_FILES`); truncation/derivation is marked honestly in `cv_hint` (`diff:full` / `diff:truncated-cap` / `diff:truncated-deadline` / `diff:unavailable`); a CREATE (`base` all-zero) → `branch-create` and a DELETE (`head` all-zero) → `ref-delete`, each with empty paths (no unbounded full-tree walk). **SSRF-safe (critical):** the webhook URL is operator config (`HUGIT_KUNGFU_WEBHOOK_URL`), MUST be `https://` (userinfo `@` refused), and MUST resolve to a PUBLIC address — loopback / link-local (incl. the `169.254.169.254` cloud-metadata IP + IPv6 analogues) / RFC-1918 / CGNAT / ULA / unspecified / multicast / documentation ranges are all REFUSED via a custom `ureq` resolver that validates every resolved IP and returns only the vetted addresses, so `ureq` connects to EXACTLY those (closing the DNS-rebind TOCTOU window); redirects are disabled (a 3xx is never followed to any target); bounded connect/request timeouts. **HMAC-signed** (`HUGIT_KUNGFU_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, HMAC-SHA256, `X-Hugit-Signature: sha256=<hex>` over the exact canonical body); the secret lives only in a `Debug`-redacted config, never in a queue item or a log line. **Fail-closed config / feature-flagged OFF by default:** an unset URL makes every emit a pure no-op (a missing config never errors a push); URL-not-https or a missing secret → DISABLED (never emit unsigned). 15 hermetic tests: real base→head diff → correct `touched_paths`; create/delete honest markers; unresolvable-oid honest-empty; HMAC against the RFC 4231 test-case-2 vector; signature over the exact canonical body; `is_global_ip` rejects every private class + accepts public; the resolver refuses private literal targets; non-https/userinfo refused; config gating fail-closed; secret redacted + never in artifacts; enqueue never blocks on a hanging sink; full-queue drops-oldest bounded at capacity; in-flight duplicate collapsed; worker delivers the signed body + idempotency key end-to-end (injected sink). Gate-green (fmt + clippy `--workspace --all-targets --locked -D warnings` + test `--workspace --locked`).
- feat(docs): **vendored hugit skills — the LLM interface to the forge** (WP W-SKILLS). hugit's primary typist is an orchestrated agent, so hugit now ships two free/open vendored skills under `skills/`: `hugit/SKILL.md` (the ORCHESTRATOR — drives the campaign→intent→pr→`land queue` flow, memoized `check`/`verdict`, and the chain-verified read surface) and `hugit-worker/SKILL.md` (the WORKER/agent — runs ONE pre-decided verb to a verified machine result and returns a compact card). Plus `skills/README.md` (the two-skill model + the three laws) and `skills/hugit/docs/MCP-CATALOG.md` (the six surfaces an agent reaches — local CLI · `/v1` reads · `/v1` writes · clone · push · the deferred runner fabric — each with its auth + honesty caveat). The skills are **ground-truth-anchored, not free prose**: the verb table mirrors `crates/hugit-cli/src/lib.rs :: HUGIT_VERBS` (live verbs + `ws`/`dispatch` marked reserved/do-not-invoke); the one error/exit law (stdout JSON, exit `0`/`2`/`1`, act on `error.fix`) mirrors `crates/hugit-cli/src/porcelain.rs`; the honesty law (built ≠ delivered · a cost is `null`-or-measured never hand-stamped · a `401`/`404` is not route-existence) is enforced in both skills, each with its own anti-pattern refusal catalog. House style mirrors the `techlead` skill template. Docs-only; no cargo gate.
- feat(mcp): **new `hugit-mcp` crate — a user-hosted MCP (Model Context Protocol) server exposing the four hugit LLM-native tools over stdio JSON-RPC 2.0** (WP-W-MCP). Ships with hugit, free/open. Minimal + honest: line-delimited JSON-RPC (`initialize` / `tools/list` / `tools/call` / `ping`; a notification gets no reply; a malformed line → a `-32700` parse-error response). A tool failure is a successful JSON-RPC response with `isError: true` (the MCP content-error envelope) so the model reads + reacts, distinct from a protocol error. The four tools, each with its honesty stated **in the tool output itself** (never just in docs): **(1) `claim-disjointness`** wraps the REAL union-engine primitive `hugit_queue::core::AffectedSet::is_disjoint` (no second source of truth) over two claims' touched-path sets, and STATES the v1 **path-approximation** in every result — disjointness is over file paths, not B3's true memoized-check blast radius, so `disjoint: true` is *necessary but not yet sufficient* (the union test remains the authority;
